the question i want to raise, mr president, is, i think, about the role and the prestige of the european parliament. at the opening ceremony of the greek presidency last wednesday in athens, there were various speeches at this presidency ceremony, there was a speech by mr van rompuy, there was a speech by mr barroso, but there was no speech, no intervention, no position, no representation from the european parliament. and the question, and the observation, is: is that what the protocol says, and will we follow it in future ceremonies that are both symbolic and substantive? the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. but that's another matter for discussion. you said you took responsibility. the question is how the european parliament is represented at these events during each country's presidency. please give me an answer if i'm wrong.

the next item is the debate on the report by chris davies, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, on the 2013 implementation report: 'development and application of carbon capture and storage technology in europe' (2013/2079 (ini)) (a7-0430/2013).
member of the commission. − mr president, first of all i should be very clear that when we talk about wildlife and crime, we are talking about organised crime and about one of the major forms of criminal activity, be it in terms of volume or in terms of consequences. these are unquestionable facts and the situation is worsening, and that is why today’s discussion is absolutely necessary and timely. there are many areas of direct relevance for the european union: biodiversity, of course, but also governance, development, international trade, transnational crime, peace and stability in certain regions, and so on. i mentioned some of them in my introductory speech. this is a problem typically linked to the globalisation of the economy and the globalisation of trade, and it needs a global response. the european union definitely has an interest in stepping up action to tackle it and in shaping the global response and, if i am frank, i am happy to see that the united states too is stepping up these types of activity. however, we should not underestimate what we are doing ourselves: preparing the communication, on which i am working together with commissioner malmström and which will be published in a month’s time; organising the conference i have mentioned; and preparing the consultation process. we are by no means inactive; on the contrary, as i mentioned, as recently as december a special new programme was announced, with financing from the european development fund, to try to address this issue. collectively, as the union, and individually in our own countries, we are among the leaders in the international community’s efforts against wildlife trafficking. i also mentioned that i am working with commissioner piebalgs, and i will continue to work with him to make sure this issue is properly taken into account, especially when we discuss the geographical allocation of funding. there are other activities too. for example, we systematically raise the question in our political and trade bilateral-relations contacts with a number of key countries, including china, vietnam and thailand. it is an area which we discuss with our us counterparts in the framework of the transatlantic trade and investment partnership, and we expect it will also be an important topic at the forthcoming meeting between the european union and the african union in april 2014. so yes, it is on our agenda. it is at the top of the political agenda, where it deserves to be, and with today’s discussion it is getting a further push. the forthcoming communication will serve to identify any gaps which need to be filled and to indicate whether there is added value in bringing the different measures under the umbrella of a single strategy or action plan, and raising their profile beyond traditional environmental circles. maybe i should also mention that the eu is the biggest donor to the international consortium on combating wildlife crime, which brings together interpol, cites, the world customs organisation, the un office on drugs and crime and the world bank. we are contributing approximately eur 1.7 million on a three-year basis. so, yes, we are aware of the problem and we are trying to tackle it. i will now turn to some specific questions which you raised. on hunting trophies, the commission is in the process of amending the rules which govern the importation into the european union of hunting trophies of some species – for example, lions – where hunting trophies might represent a threat to their sustainability, given the level of imports in freight. the same is true for elephants and rhinos, so the commission will soon propose an amendment to the regulation for those species. with this change, the authorities in charge of implementing cites in our member states will have to check whether an import is sustainable before authorising it. if they consider that this is not the case, no import permit will be delivered and the import will not take place. regarding support for the detection of online trade, eu enforcement officers are already coordinating action to improve the investigation of trafficking, including internet trade. the consultation process will address this and will identify whether additional steps are needed. finally, concerning data, enforcement groups meet twice a year and, yes, we collect data from member states on enforcement, prosecutions and so on. we have an annual reporting process, so we are not starting from scratch. we are pretty busy, but it is clear that criminal activity in this area is increasing and that it warrants more of our attention as well as new, innovative methods. we will not find the answer only from an environmental angle: that is absolutely clear, because we are talking about a major form of organised crime. this is why i am working together with my colleagues, commissioners malmström, georgieva, piebalgs and de gucht, and with baroness ashton: we are working together because there is no other way to find an effective solution to these criminal activities. thank you for the debate today and for the attention you are giving to this terrible problem.
mr president, the wrong policy pursued by the troika and the greek governments and the collapse of the economy have increased the number of uninsured citizens. today, the uninsured greek citizens, not counting migrants and asylum seekers, number three million, almost 30% of the population. this is not in keeping with the european social model and european values, that is to say, losing access to health services to people, mainly self-employed people, who for years or decades have paid their insurance contributions regularly and who are now suddenly thrown to the margins because of the crisis because they are unable to pay. many citizens - when they have a problem - no longer go to doctors on time, do not buy medicines, and even more so, those who have health problems have difficulty in getting continuous treatment. the european commission must now work with the social and professional bodies to find a solution to ensure that all citizens have access to health services, whether they are unemployed or unable to pay their social security contributions.

mr president, the development of a strategy and action plan for research and innovation in the next programming period is a priority for funding from the european regional development fund, but at the same time a very large source of opportunities. but how will this strategy be shaped? it must be shaped through a dialogue with the social partners in each region, there must be coordination between the regions and the member states, but also an exchange of information and best practice between the regions in order to close the gap that is important between the different regions today. the european commission must also step up these efforts and, of course, the regions must be more closely involved in the seville platform. a good way of strengthening regional action is to place great emphasis on the involvement of young researchers, and also students, in research and innovation efforts, so that they can be a bridge to the future and promote new ideas for regional development.
in writing. the eu in the new programming period (2014-2020) is putting a strong emphasis on promoting research and innovation. the horizon programme is the cornerstone of the european strategy to improve europe's competitiveness, but individual structural funds can also be used to this end. in practice, however, this dual approach raises questions about the coordination of the approaches of the structural funds and horizon 2020 as they have a completely different structure and structure. for this reason, the financial provisions provide inter alia that a project may, under certain conditions, be supported by more than one fund. the report also goes one step further and, although i voted in favour of it, it recognises and promotes the support of local authorities and regions by providing advice to avoid confusion which would deprive valuable resources for development and support for employment in local communities.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report because it argues that intelligent specialisation means that, when developing strategies for intelligent regional innovation, regions should prioritise the funding of research, development and innovation in accordance with their comparative advantages. there should also be a close link between research and higher education institutions, enterprises (especially smes) and local and regional administrations. this should be a two-way process, taking into account new developments in innovation and exploiting the potential synergies of different funding options at local, regional, national and international level. in addition, the proposal to create a european commission guide to the question of financing a project from different sources is considered interesting. it should be stressed that the timing of the report is important, as the regions are in the process of preparing their programmes, including research, development and innovation strategies, for the 2014-2020 programming period. the report can therefore in its own way contribute to these efforts.
in writing. the report, which i supported, does not only deal with food safety and consumer interests, which are at the heart of the european parliament's concerns. the focus this time is on the issue of food fraud as a result of false labelling and other fraud cases that have affected the eu food chain. road salt used in food and false labelling of organic products are just two of the key examples for which the report proposes to further investigate the issue of food fraud, concluding, inter alia, on the need to define what constitutes food fraud, to strengthen the role of the wto in identifying cases of food fraud, to increase cooperation between member states through europol and to impose sanctions at least double the amount of the economic benefit sought through food fraud.
in writing. this own-initiative report, which i voted in favour of, focuses on the readiness of the member states for the next period of funding for cohesion policy. the information available to the european parliament shows that the member states are not at a similar stage of preparation. changes in the preparation of the operational programmes by the member states compared to the previous funding period, such as structural changes in the operational programmes, play an important role in this. however, for better planning and therefore for more efficient use of european resources it is important for greece as well to attempt a detailed examination and evaluation of successful and failed efforts of the 2007-2013 programming period, in order to avoid repeating mistakes and to eliminate those administrative or non-administrative obstacles that led to insufficient use of resources from the european structural funds. it is also noted that some member states are already undertaking this form of self-assessment in total, making it much easier to prepare their strategy for the new programming period.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report by mr derek vaughan, which examines whether the member states are prepared for the new period of cohesion policy. for a number of reasons, such as the specificities of the member states and the situation prevailing in the context of the current economic situation, member states are at very different stages in the process of preparing partnership agreements and operational programmes. for this reason, it is considered particularly useful to cooperate and exchange best practices among them. the european parliament has worked hard to ensure that the negotiations with the council on the cohesion legislative package and the mff were concluded in time to shape the regulatory framework and determine the funding for this policy. the commission's proposal for a regulation on the establishment of a european agency for the evaluation of medicinal products for human use (hereinafter 'the agency') is based on the principle of equal treatment for all medicinal products for human use. it is now up to the member states, in close cooperation with local and regional actors, to finalise the drafting of the agreements to enable the programmes to be implemented, which will contribute to the member states' efforts at economic recovery and development.
madam president, we agree with julie girling on many issues, but we usually have different positions on gmo issues, and this is a gmo issue. what we have in front of us is the choice between, on the one hand, a commission proposal which in practice does not respect the decision of the european court of justice and does not allow for labelling of gmo honey and, on the other, a proposal which allows us to have labelling to say whether the honey is produced by bees which have collected pollen from gm crops. it is very simple: if we vote for the commission proposal, which is also julie girling’s proposal, we are voting in favour of not having gm labelling for honey which is produced by bees that live in the middle of miles and miles of gm cultivation. if we vote for what mr tabajdi is proposing – the s&d’s proposal – we are voting for a clear labelling procedure for honey which is produced by bees that have used gm crops to collect their pollen. so the choice is very clear. (the speaker agreed to take a blue-card question under rule 149(8))
blue-card answer. – thank you very much for the question. it is very clear, and i agree with mr peterle, that we need to have labelling. if we need to name something as an ingredient in order to have gm labelling and to be able to inform citizens about what they are eating, let us do it. this is not about ingredients or natural constituents, it is actually about labelling. do you yourself agree that we need to label gm honey?
madam president, when the european commission announced last september its approval of the amendment to the honey directive to clarify the true nature of pollen as a natural ingredient, it assured us that the proposed amendment would not affect the european court of justice's conclusion on pollen as regards the application of the legislation on genetically modified organisms to food. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them. the commission has also proposed that the commission should be given the task of drawing up a report on the implementation of the directive on the protection of the environment in the event of a natural disaster. since honey is a natural product, it does not need to be accompanied by a list of ingredients. however, it is considered necessary that honey, as well as other foodstuffs or food supplements containing pollen derived from genetically modified crops, should be labelled as containing ingredients produced from genetically modified organisms. we also believe that the european commission should not be given the power to amend the technical characteristics relating to the names, descriptions of products, their definitions and the criteria for their composition by means of delegated acts. the commission's proposal for a regulation on genetically modified organisms, which is based on the principle of free choice for consumers, coexistence and zero tolerance, is designed to restore legal certainty for honey producers and beekeepers, as well as for importers and, of course, first and foremost, for consumers.
blue-card question. – i would like to ask you whether you know that, under this proposal, any beekeeper whose hives are at a distance of 10 kilometres or more from any gm cultivation will not be required to bear any cost of controls. these controls will entail a cost only for beekeepers who have hives within or very close to gm cultivations.
in writing. hercule iii is the programme established to provide technical equipment and training to increase efficiency in the fight against fraud, corruption and other illegal activities (such as smuggling, counterfeiting) which are also detrimental to the eu budget. in the final compromise text, the cor managed to include some of the most fundamental issues it supported, such as the coordination of the programme at eu level, the establishment of detailed performance indicators to measure the specific objectives and the preparation of a more detailed annual work programme and an interim report, and a thorough final evaluation of the programme's achievements and added value. it is also of particular importance that the co-financing rate be set at 80% and that it be increased to 90% in the event that the member states are exposed to a high risk affecting the protection of the financial interests of the union. in the present period of budgetary constraints and the rationalisation of public finances, both at national and european level, it is necessary to make full use of existing union funding. we must therefore safeguard the union's financial interests by ensuring that not a single euro is lost to illegal activities.

in writing. i voted against the mixing of honey with genetically modified organisms (gmos) and in favour of mandatory labelling of genetically modified pollen in honey. the consumer must be able to know whether the honey he chooses contains genetically modified (gm) pollen. it is important to protect the unique quality of greek honey and the 20 000 greek beekeepers.
in writing. i voted in favour of the final compromise text of the regulation on the hercule iii financial programme, which was specifically designed to combat fraud, corruption and any other illegal activity affecting the eu's financial interests, such as smuggling and counterfeiting. it is particularly important that the european parliament has managed to include in the final text provisions which help to make the programme more effective and results-oriented. the fact that the co-financing rate is set at 80% and can be increased to 90% in the event that member states are exposed to a high risk affecting the protection of the union's financial interests is also a major relief for those member states subject to budgetary constraints. the protection of the union's financial interests is a matter of major importance, particularly in the light of the ongoing efforts to rationalise the budget, as the union is called upon to respond to its enhanced role and increased responsibilities with resources that are limited compared to the past. in this context, it is imperative that money from the eu budget is not lost, and we must also guarantee that european taxpayers' money is not at risk of being squandered through fraud, corruption or other illegal activities.

madam president, commissioner, in the europe of 2014, homelessness is unfortunately becoming a plague. so while the fight against homelessness is a key priority for the union, the numbers of homeless people are still increasing in the member states, and particularly in countries with memoranda of understanding, such as my own country. when we speak of the homeless, we are talking about the "newly homeless", people of a different profile, family heads who are suddenly condemned to poverty because the consequences of a crisis, such as extreme austerity, unemployment at historically high rates, a sharp decline in wages with rising energy costs, household costs and overtaxation, form a grim reality that threatens to make hundreds of thousands of our fellow citizens homeless, under the threat of foreclosures and auctions, if they are unable to meet their obligations, either due to borrowing under previous conditions or to state services. according to a recent study, commissioner, it is estimated that for every euro spent on preventing homelessness, 2.2 euros of public expenditure arising from the effects of the problem is saved. in this context, and given that oligarchy has social, economic and, above all, humanitarian costs, immediate action and decisive solutions are needed, and i therefore wish to call for special care to be taken to protect our vulnerable fellow citizens against seizures and evictions; and, on the other hand, to facilitate greece, the only country without a social housing network, to use european funds in this direction, which will bring significant benefits to vulnerable citizens above all, but also to society and the real economy in general.

in writing. intelligent grids are modernised electricity networks that use analogue or digital technology and communication information to gather information and act in an automated manner to improve the efficiency, reliability, economy and sustainability of electricity generation and distribution. the development of smart grids requires a stable and long-term policy framework. member states and regions are therefore encouraged to invest as early as possible in smart local networks, making use of the european structural and investment funds. it is also important that local and regional authorities and citizens are involved in all phases of the design, implementation and conversion of energy networks. in addition, active participation of distribution service operators and providers of smart grid technologies is necessary. for a future more energy-efficient, europe's regions should strive to become energy producers rather than consumers. this will be beneficial for all eu strategic objectives, including those linked to the creation and maintenance of jobs and the protection of the environment.
in writing. small farms, although they do not make a large contribution to the total agricultural product, are of great importance for the environment and regional cohesion. it will be in everyone's interest to help the smallest to develop dynamism and entrepreneurship, to become aware of the particularly great environmental challenges of our day and to be facilitated to develop a different type of competition from that associated with economies of scale. so we should aim for the following: • promoting the demographic renewal of the sector and access to broadband networks; • promoting the linking of agricultural activities with processing and the provision of services (e.g. tourism), in a way that upgrades and highlights the environment and cultural heritage of each region; • ensuring that small farms are well informed about good practices and that small-scale production is linked to effective alternative marketing methods. the advisory system provided for by the reform of the common agricultural policy is a good start, but there is no guarantee of quality in its implementation in each member state.
in writing. - i voted in favour. this proposal for a council decision laying down rules and procedures to enable the participation of greenland in the kimberley process certification scheme (com (2013) 0429) is closely connected to the proposal for a regulation amending council regulation (ec) no 2368/2002 as regards the inclusion of greenland in implementing the kimberley process certification scheme. together these proposals aim at enabling greenland to participate in the kimberley process certification scheme (kpcs) on rough diamonds through its cooperation with the european union. the scheme set out in the proposal would allow for the import and export of rough diamonds from greenland to the union as well as to other participants in the certification scheme, provided that all imports and exports of rough diamonds are verified and, for exports, certified by union authorities pursuant to the rules set out in regulation (ec) no 2368/2002 implementing the kimberley process certification scheme for the international trade in rough diamonds.

mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, mr commissioner, the single resolution mechanism and the single resolution fund are integral parts of the banking union. what needs does the banking union meet, in line with the approach of the european commission, which the european parliament has welcomed from the outset? the need to overcome the current fragmentation in the european banking system, which is recycling the crisis, especially in the economically distressed countries, but also the rupture of the umbilical cord of banks and countries in which they are based, an element that creates conditions of great inequality - as we experience in greece - in the financing of the real economy. these are the problems which the banking union in general and the single resolution mechanism in particular have to address. but this objective is not served if decisions on the single resolution mechanism are taken hostage by governments, hostage by national vetoes, recycling internal contradictions and institutional disparities between strong and less powerful countries, and it is not served if the single resolution fund is relegated to greek calendar and left weakened outside the european framework. mr president, you have said - and quite rightly - that the market, the citizens, the depositors are waiting for the resolution mechanism and that the agreement must be concluded soon, and i am sure that the greek presidency will do its best to achieve this. we want an agreement, but not just any agreement, because if the resolution mechanism does not work properly, there is a risk that we will achieve the opposite of the desired result and, instead of safeguarding financial stability, keep it in a state of permanent destabilisation and constant risk.
mr president, mr president-in-office of the council, mr commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i consider it very important that the time has finally come for the institutions to discuss among themselves the banking union, on specific plans and proposals, and in particular the single resolution mechanism, which is the heart of this plan and is also the most thorny issue. we all now realise that the banking union is essential for the functioning of the single market, for the balanced development of the north and the south, for the protection of depositors and taxpayers. let's not miss this moment. we are called upon today to give real substance to this plan. the european parliament's proposals are in that direction and we call for constructive cooperation with the council to achieve a real banking union. as my colleagues have already said, our aim is for the single resolution mechanism to be a european mechanism with effective resources, procedures and instruments. the european union and its citizens have suffered greatly from reckless actions and half-measures. the european union must not be trapped in its weaknesses, and the intergovernmental procedure proposed by the council is one of those weaknesses of the union. i hope, mr president, that you will be given a mandate by the council, that you will have its support and that together with the european parliament we will chart a european path towards the banking union with effective instruments for stability, cohesion and growth.

mr president, commissioner, every year some 500 million tonnes of cargo are transported by inland waterways. this volume is equivalent to the transport of 25 million lorries and 140 billion tonne-kilometres of freight. that's the great thing about river transport. they offer major advantages in transport performance, sustainability and external costs. a well-maintained network of waterways, free of bottlenecks and missing links, is therefore essential to complete the integration of inland navigation and to ensure its reliability. during naiades i, the network of waterways was not adequately maintained, nor were the missing links completed, in many cases due to national budget cuts. we therefore welcome the new naiades ii package of measures presented by the commission to make better use of the potential of inland waterways. the commission proposes structural changes in the inland waterway sector, with improvements in quality, operating conditions and infrastructure, and the integration of the sector into the supply chain. however, the commission does not propose any innovative specific measures, such as appropriate and specific funding, which would ensure the full implementation of the action programme. naiades ii seems to be a jumbled combination of existing measures with no real priorities and no real hierarchies. aware of the importance of the inland waterway sector and of ensuring a sustainable transport chain, i am concerned about the lack of a stable and specific strategy for the inland waterway sector accompanied by adequate funding. we therefore look forward to a new, more ambitious proposal and we hope that the potential of the new transeuropean networks will be exploited through the facilitating europe budget and the horizon 2020 programme.
in writing. the commission's green paper and the european parliament's report on the 'eu 2030 climate and energy policy framework' echo the ambitions of the monopolies to create new areas of profitable activity, with renewable energy and 'green technologies' as their main focus, with a focus on climate change. the debate over the size of the emission reductions, the share of renewable energy, the mandatory or non-mandatory nature of eu policy objectives, the promotion of all or part of them is a fierce one between sectors of capital active in energy-related and energy-related sectors and industries. competitions within the eu, as well as between the eu and other imperialist centres and powerful capitalist economies for the means of production, control of energy sources and transport routes, can only bring harm to the people. the condition for energy to be a social good, a lever for satisfying the needs of the people, is that the working people be freed from the domination of the monopolies and the shackles of their imperialist associations, to conquer power and make the monopolies popular property, so that they can enjoy the wealth they produce.

mr president, today's debate is not a routine debate, it is a substantive and very important debate. i would like to say that i am very pleased that the commission has taken the initiative of presenting this report to the house. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. they are confronted every day with the unemployment rate, the infant mortality rate, the poverty rate, the drop-out rate. the reports by the three colleagues, mr cofferati, mr gutiérrez prieto and mr de backer, give, i think, very specific 'advice', if i may use the term, to the commission. the commission has also set itself three very specific objectives: to strengthen the social dimension of the european and monetary policy, to strengthen the social pillar, and to ensure that we, as the european parliament and the european commission, finally take account not only of economic but also of social indicators. i also propose something which i think might help: in future, a joint meeting of the ministers of economic affairs and social affairs should be established on a permanent basis.
in writing. i voted in favour of this draft report as it is linked to a proposal covering 160 legislative acts in various policy areas. the rapporteur suggested that the committees consider these three proposals as one package with the same timetable and adopt them as soon as possible, so that the alignment process could be completed, ideally, by the end of the current parliamentary term. in addition, the european parliament and the council will have the right to indicate the basic acts which they consider to require priority adaptation.

in writing. i voted in favour of this draft report as it is linked to a proposal covering 160 legislative acts in various policy areas. the rapporteur suggested that the committees consider these three proposals as one package with the same timetable and adopt them as soon as possible, so that the alignment process could be completed, ideally, by the end of the current parliamentary term. in addition, the european parliament and the council will have the right to indicate the basic acts which they consider to require priority adaptation.
in writing. i voted in favour of this long-awaited proposal for the revision of the trade mark system in europe, which was submitted by the commission at the end of march 2013, after many years of work. the quality of the legislative process cannot be compromised and the opportunity offered by this revision to modernise the trademark system in europe should not be lost in the pursuit of a rapid agreement between the institutions.
in writing. the report on the regulation on the freezing and confiscation of the instruments and proceeds of crime in the european union is another step towards the harmonisation of the criminal rules of the national laws of the member states, towards the establishment of a common eu criminal law. the eu is again exploiting issues that are widely accepted by workers, such as the confiscation of assets from bribes of public officials, etc., to manipulate public consciences, to hijack consensus on the adoption of common criminal rules by the eu, in order to later extend them to more and more areas, creating its own criminal framework that will prevail over the national criminal law of the member states. the bourgeois classes of the member states are increasingly giving the eu institutions and its repressive mechanisms powers and responsibilities that touch the "hard core" of the bourgeois state, because they believe that the union of monopolies has the combined power of the monopolies to shield their power against the workers' and popular movement, the popular indignation that is fuelled by the barbaric anti-popular attack of capital on the working class and the popular strata. that is why the peoples must oppose their own coalition of will against the eu and the monopolies it serves.
in writing. i voted in favour of the proposal for a directive on this subject, as the adoption of these minimum rules on the freezing and confiscation of the proceeds of crime in the european union will harmonise the member states' freezing and confiscation regimes, enhancing mutual trust and facilitating effective crossborder cooperation. it will also be a step towards enhancing the mutual recognition of freezing and confiscation orders, which are an important aspect of the fight against cross-border serious and organised crime in the eu.
in writing. i voted in favour of this long-awaited proposal for the revision of the trade mark system in europe, which was submitted by the commission at the end of march 2013, after many years of work. the quality of the legislative process cannot be compromised and the opportunity offered by this revision to modernise the trademark system in europe should not be lost in the pursuit of a rapid agreement between the institutions.
in writing. the new regulation on the operation and functioning of europol greatly strengthens its repressive role. greater interconnection and information exchange is ensured not only with the intelligence and law enforcement agencies of each eu member state, but also with the corresponding agencies of third countries, such as the usa. the extension of its mission beyond 'terrorism' and 'organised crime' to all forms of 'serious crime' facilitates the extension of its repressive function to actions of the labour 'popular' movement, which is also the main objective of all repressive mechanisms that systematically structure the eu and governments in a giant network. the monitoring of practically everything and without exception is the most characteristic element of the extension of its action and its powers, which are guaranteed by the new regulation. the amendments to the european parliament's report on the alleged 'protection of personal data' and 'parliamentary accountability' of europol are a hoax and an alibi to legitimize its repressive action and the dossier, since they neither ensure any effective protection and control, nor do the nature and functioning of these mechanisms to shield the power of monopolies allow any control.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report because the last decade has seen an increase in serious and organised crime in the eu and a diversification of the forms it takes. according to europol's 2013 serious and organised crime risk assessment (socta 2013), 'serious and organised crime is constantly growing stronger and more complex, and remains a serious threat to security and prosperity in the eu'. the study also notes that 'the impact of globalisation on society and business has facilitated the emergence of new forms of criminal activity, with criminal networks exploiting loopholes in legislation, the internet and the conditions associated with the economic crisis to make illicit profits with little risk'. the internet is used for the organisation and execution of criminal activities as a communication tool, a trading venue, a recruitment platform and a financial services mechanism. it also facilitates innovative forms of cybercrime, credit card fraud and the distribution of child sexual abuse material. for all these reasons, a european police office characterised by effective and efficient cooperation and exchange of information is essential.
in writing. the report has nothing to do with the principles of solidarity and friendship between peoples. the eu seeks to exploit the moods and solidarity of young people to create half-wage, half-professional youth corps to provide an alibi and support for its imperialist interventions across the globe, which it plans and executes, sometimes under the guise of 'humanitarian disasters', sometimes under the guise of 'conflict prevention' or 'crisis management'. it is an attempt to bring young people into these imperialist missions who will provide free services to its dirty aims, to gain positions for the monopolies it represents, to exploit the peoples and the wealth of their countries. the vulgarity of its aims is expressed openly in the regulation, which even establishes 'certificates of eligibility' for the 'volunteer' participants, as 'passport' for the various eu-funded ngos and governments, to serve their imperialist ambitions under the cloak of these organisations which are the long arm of the eu's imperialist interventions. young people must reject this rottenness, this mire that the eu and governments are trying to drag them into. the european parliament has already expressed its views on the situation in the former yugoslavia, and we are therefore asking for the commission to take the necessary steps to ensure that the situation in the former yugoslavia is not further aggravated by the events in the former yugoslavia.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report as it aims to express the union's humanitarian values and solidarity towards those in need through the promotion of an effective and visible european voluntary humanitarian aid corps, which contributes to strengthening the union's capacity to respond to humanitarian crises and to building the capacity and resilience of vulnerable or disaster-affected communities in third countries.
in writing. i voted for it. the support sought by this proposed fund should not be seen as a substitute for the integrated policies needed to reduce and ultimately eradicate poverty. this continues to be a challenge for both the union and the member states. the resources proposed for this fund are extremely limited and far from sufficient. the aim of the project is to develop a european network of experts in the field of social protection, to provide information on the social and economic impact of the implementation of the social protection system and to provide a platform for the exchange of information on social protection and on the social and economic impact of social protection systems. focusing on people at risk of material and food deprivation, in particular the homeless, children and households with children, and on accompanying social inclusion measures will address some of the worst and most severe forms of deprivation and also enhance existing and planned eu efforts in these areas.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report as the biocidal products regulation represents a significant and substantial revision of the existing legislation governing biocidal products by harmonising the rules on the placing on the market and use of biocidal products while ensuring a high level of protection of human and animal health and the environment.
in writing. the reports on the "european semester" voted by the grand alliance of capital in the european parliament - the people's party, social democrats, liberals - reflect the demand of the monopolies for even greater intensity of the anti-people attack against the working class and popular strata throughout the eu. the political staff of the plutocracy is calling for the unwavering implementation, without any relaxation, of capitalist restructuring in every eu member state, with or without a memorandum, regardless of budget deficits and debt, to further reduce the price of labour power in order to ensure monopoly profitability. it calls on the eu and its institutions, the bourgeois governments, the parties of capital and the euro-path, to tighten the anti-people chains even more, under the central guidance and supervision of the eu. the common concern of all the political forces serving the bourgeois administration, whatever mix they propose, is how to enhance the 'competitiveness' of the european union groups by intensifying the exploitation of workers. the peoples can turn the agony of the bourgeoisie into a nightmare by strengthening the communist parties that oppose the eu.
mr president, commissioner, commercial air transport is celebrating its centenary this year and the industry can boast that aviation safety is now at an all-time high worldwide. in europe, in fact, the data show that we have achieved the highest level of security in the world. in 2013 there were no fatal accidents in easa member states, despite 6 million commercial flights and more than 800 million passengers carried. the challenge of aviation safety is not about the present, but about the future. the number of flights is expected to double by 2030. the enormous capacity problems already observed in europe's airspace and airports will reach a peak, creating real threats to flight safety. in the face of such a scenario, we can only take precautions today to ensure the safety of the millions of citizens who choose to fly every year. the text agreed with the council will help to harmonise aviation safety elements and will strengthen the role of the community central database and of easa. it is an important step which will naturally add to the great contribution to security that cesar technologies will have from 2020 onwards. i would like to congratulate the rapporteur. thank you very much.
i would like to congratulate mrs sehnalová on her excellent report. the ecall system already exists and is used, but not at pan-european level. it's a technology that is estimated to save 2,500 lives a year, so it's a measure that will make a significant contribution to our goal of reducing fatal accidents by 50 percent by 2020. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's transport policy is not disrupted by the introduction of new technologies. obviously, there are many factors that lead to the loss of life on asphalt. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.

on behalf of the efd group. the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat unemployment, including the creation of a youth guarantee fund. it's good, but not much. i am afraid that in order to combat the problem of unemployment, it is necessary to focus our attention both on improving the business climate in europe and on the link between the labour market and education. it is not possible that there are so many millions of unemployed people with higher education degrees and we are getting messages from the business world that they lack qualified staff. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the social partners are able to take part in the negotiations. to end and replace the practice of repetitive pre-training programmes that facilitate entrepreneurs to hire highly qualified staff at low cost. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a welcome step towards the creation of a european employment strategy.
commissioner, this weekend i was in three different cities in greece: the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to finance the necessary measures. i spoke to them there about the youth guarantee initiative. there are high expectations on the part of our young people, as there are high expectations on the part of the local communities. you yourself will be in another greek city, kavala, next week. we are making efforts as greece, despite the recession, to make use of the youth guarantee, and we have succeeded. forty thousand jobs will be created in 2014; the amount to be allocated is eur 340 million, half of which comes from the youth initiative and the other half from the european social fund. but there are difficulties, commissioner. the difficulties are the recession that is knocking on greece's door permanently, not to say that it is in greece's living room. the difficulty is that 90% of firms have fewer than 10 employees and therefore it is difficult to create apprenticeship or job opportunities. we want more! the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we want more. the president interrupts the speaker.
in writing. i voted in favour of the own-initiative report on outermost regions, the structural funds and other eu programmes, a report which recognises all the problems associated with the outermost regions and recalls the need for a real and profound change in the new programming period. the eu should make better use of the advantages and opportunities that the outermost regions can offer the union, as in issues such as tackling climate change, research and technology and the union's relations with third countries, these regions are central to the union's development plans and prospects. on the other hand, the eu has an interest in supporting these regions, which suffer from serious and permanent physical and demographic problems which hamper their development. therefore, it is essential to have and promote synergies between the structural funds and all other union funds, strategies and policies in order to fulfil the prospects of both the union and the outermost regions.
in writing. i voted in favour of the own-initiative report on outermost regions, the structural funds and other eu programmes, a report which recognises all the problems associated with the outermost regions and recalls the need for a real and profound change in the new programming period. the eu should make better use of the advantages and opportunities that the outermost regions can offer the union, as in issues such as tackling climate change, research and technology and the union's relations with third countries, these regions are central to the union's development plans and prospects. on the other hand, the eu has an interest in supporting these regions, which suffer from serious and permanent physical and demographic problems which hamper their development. therefore, it is essential to have and promote synergies between the structural funds and all other union funds, strategies and policies in order to fulfil the prospects of both the union and the outermost regions.
in writing. according to article 318 of the lisbon treaty, the european commission is required to 'present to the european parliament and the council an evaluation report on the union's finances based on the results achieved', in particular in relation to the discharge procedure. for the financial year 2012, the european commission presented the third evaluation report taking into account the various recommendations of the european parliament in the previous two reports, including the link between the different eu programmes and the objectives of the europe 2020 strategy. i voted in favour of the own-initiative report on the evaluation of the union's finances, which is a response to the european commission's report. it is of particular importance that the european commission report explains in a simple and concise manner how eu spending contributes to the achievement of the europe 2020 objectives at union and global level. in addition, the european court of auditors should monitor the progress made by the commission in the design and operation of its risk management and internal control procedures, and make recommendations if any shortcomings are identified, and report on these issues in the commission's report.
in writing. the kke voted against all the regulations that make up the so-called '4th package' for the single european railway area, which is a single package that completes the full privatisation of rail transport, networks and infrastructure in the european union. the handing over of railways, all the public infrastructure associated with them, to monopolies serves to enhance the 'competitiveness' - the profitability of business groups - that the eu serves. it is a major blow not only to working conditions and labour rights but also to the railway workers' right to strike itself. it is a serious deterioration in the quality and safety of rail transport, and it has raised fares to the detriment of the working class. the workers, the labour movement in the eu countries must draw conclusions about the role and character of the eu, the union of this capital. to be effective, the struggles must target the power of the monopolies, confront the eu itself and its anti-people structure, and be directed towards the goal of breaking free from its anti-people chains, with radical changes in power and the economy.
in writing. the kke opposes the so-called 'third programme of action of the union in the field of health 2014-2020' because it further promotes the anti-people policy of the eu and the urban governments of the member states in the field of health. it reinforces the notion of "individual responsibility" for health and "competitiveness", i.e. the greater penetration of monopoly groups in health, resulting in the greater degradation of public health systems, the payment of health services - whether in the public or private sector - from the backlog of the popular family. this policy, which is applied in a uniform manner in all eu member states, leads public health systems to compete with business groups, to "sell" health services, which are now paid for by workers, putting their own hand deep in their pocket, but also through their insurance funds, which are transformed into customers of either businessmen or public health structures. the workers' and people's movement must intensify its struggle against the commercialisation of health promoted in all eu member states and governments, to demand the development of a single, exclusively public and completely free health system, with the main orientation being the prevention and abolition of all business activity.
in writing. the kke voted against the report on the proposal for a regulation on transeuropean telecommunications networks, because it promotes the completion of the eu's single capital market, privatisation in the telecommunications and digital services sectors. fifty billion euros, hot money from the eu budget, looted from the people's income, are destined to go into the pockets of the monopoly giants of telecommunications, digital networks, internet service providers, the information technology industry, for the development of high-speed broadband networks, so that their profitability is guaranteed even in areas where the investment does not benefit business groups, remote and sparsely populated peripheries, island regions. the development of digital infrastructures will benefit the monopoly capital as a whole, which will gain access to new markets within the eu single market. the big losers will again be the working class and the popular strata who will either be forced to pay double and overpriced for the infrastructure and use of digital services, or will remain excluded from them. it is once again confirmed that the satisfaction of the people's needs is in conflict with the eu, the alliance of monopolies, and requires its overthrow.
mr president, after the tragic days ukraine has been through, everyone, inside and outside the country, must treat this matter with seriousness and absolute responsibility. the european union must not repeat the mistakes it made in the balkans in the 1990s. first, we must guarantee and facilitate the holding of free and fair elections as soon as possible. it should also be made clear that the union does not identify with specific sections of the ukrainian opposition, nor does it consider extremist pro-nazi nationalist forces to be legitimate representatives. the parties in the european parliament have a responsibility to safeguard the legitimacy of the transitional government and no one, but no one, has the right to exploit the dynamics of the situation for temporary party benefits. secondly, the territorial integrity of ukraine must be safeguarded. a series of articles in the international press referring to the so-called artificial unit are misleading. the unity in ukraine is no more artificial than in other european countries. thirdly, we must ensure that russia is actively involved in finding a solution, otherwise there can be no solution. the european union is also committed to the principle of subsidiarity, which is the basis for the european union's policy on the environment. )
in writing. the situation in ukraine is, in my view, more dangerous than any other situation that has arisen in the last three years (syria, arab spring, iran). the situation is developing in the russian hinterland and it is understood that the russians have every interest in securing their own influence in the region; the european union is also committed to the development of the region, and the region of crimea is host to large russian military and naval forces. i am afraid that russia must also realize that there are also vested interests of europe and the west in general in the issue of 'ukraine' and both sides should, i imagine, replace the power, which at the moment is in a vacuum, by serious political forces and not by supporting either extremist or fraudulent male and female. the european union (eu) has so far shown no courage. it is certain that there is poverty among the ukrainian people. on the other hand, russia, as a major energy supplier, has the potential to give ukraine's economy a much better breathing space than the eu is proposing, by simply discounting the price of the energy resources it supplies to ukraine. i therefore believe that the eu, aware of this problem, should be very bold in its proposals for financial aid to ukraine.
mr president, we are now very close to an era in which drones will be part of the defence infrastructure of every state. it is therefore necessary for the european union to have a common policy on the context in which they are used. the view is that these aircraft are instruments of peace and security. there is no doubt that their use is multifaceted and extremely beneficial in many areas beyond defence, such as mapping, border control, firefighting and agriculture. but unfortunately, in the last decade we have seen the widespread use of armed drones for extrajudicial targeted killings in the territories of third countries. such operations are carried out under a veil of secrecy, mainly by the united states of america, and outside the framework of international legality. the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the use of the term 'competent authority' to cover the whole of the european union. in order to prevent a repetition of humanitarian tragedies such as those that have occurred in recent years, notably in pakistan and yemen, the union should oppose such practices openly and clearly, should call for greater transparency in the use of armed drones by third countries and should reach a common position on their use in the light of respect for human rights and international humanitarian law. the military use of drones does not raise new factual issues under international humanitarian law and the law of armed conflict. technology is not the key to solving the problem. the only solution is to use it properly.
madam president, the very fact that the use of unmanned combat aerial vehicles is being discussed, rather than a complete ban, is further proof of the reactionary nature of the european union. it seems that the thousands of innocent people who have already been killed by these weapons, and by illegal flights from the coast of sicily, are not enough. the development of new lethal weapons systems is part of the decisions of the european union summit, which are intensifying its imperialist aggression. the european union is seeking to develop the manufacture and use of such weapons systems by its war industries and the consortium promoted by france, germany, greece, italy, the netherlands, poland and spain, and aims at the unimpeded use of airspace and the future activation of these weapons against the peoples of the member states as well. billions of euros from the plundering of the people's income will be directed to the monopolies of the war industry, to war research programmes, to the improvement of the european union's satellite and other systems necessary for the new weapons, at the same time that the european union is dismantling the greek defense industry because it does not respond to the ambitions of the monopolies. the achievements of science and technology are not only not being used to satisfy the needs of the people but, on the contrary, are being used to sow death and destruction in the ruthless competition of the imperialists for profit and influence... (the president interrupts)
on behalf of the efd group. the report by the rapporteur on a subject as important as human rights is full of enthusiasm, contains many positive elements but also some - i will not say negative - unrealistic elements. in particular, apart from our opposition to free access to drugs and our questions on euthanasia, paragraph 42 of the report refers to the wider principle of migration and the principle of non-refoulement. the principle of the return of illegal immigrants and the conclusion of readmission agreements are the most practical tools available to prevent and address illegal immigration and should also be a permanent element of the european union's external relations with third countries. that's where we disagree! moreover, the above is guaranteed by laws and treaties of increased formal validity vis-à-vis that exposure. in addition, some of the points in the report, such as those on freezing mechanisms in member states' legislative procedures and the drawing up of scoreboards, i consider to be outside the scope of the european parliament's competence and the principle of proportionality must be respected. i think it is important, in a time of economic crisis, to focus our attention on safeguarding the rights to work, social welfare and health. we do not need to go far to third countries; the european union, with its budget cuts and in other memorandum countries, is currently doing very poorly in protecting fundamental human rights for the most vulnerable groups of the population.
rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on development. - mr[nbsp ]president, i am pleased that we are discussing here a flegt (forest law governance, enforcement and trade) agreement with a very important country, indonesia, which possesses one of the most important rainforests in the world – albeit a rainforest that is very quickly becoming depleted. the flegt agreement is crucial because it gives some hope for a change in the rhythm of depletion of this important natural resource. a number of specific issues have been mentioned by the rapporteur, mr[nbsp ]jadot, and are included in the report. i would call on the commission to take very close note of the points made with regard to the indonesian timber legality assurance system: how it is malfunctioning, and the challenges in order for it to function properly and for the flegt agreement to work properly. i would like to take a moment to praise the flegt agreements as a form of european policy instrument. unlike many other trade agreements we have, the flegt agreements are, in a way, bilateral trade agreements, which respect the sovereign rights of the nations concerned. as a model of broad stakeholder participation, they could be followed by other types of trade agreement with other partners. on a negative note, however, in my capacity as parliament’s rapporteur on the commission green paper for forest protection, i must mention that the commission, in this green paper, has neglected all of parliament’s proposals. it has taken no account of parliament’s plans on any of the legal tools that parliament asked it to introduce. i would like to hear a reply from the commissioner on this point.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, we are all very well aware of the developments and the situation in venezuela, but not of course the western media, which presents the facts in a biased way in favour of the opposition. they even went so far as to post pictures of scenes of violence by the repressive forces in greece and claim that these are taking place in caracas. we know the situation in venezuela from the social work that is being done there, namely reducing unemployment, eliminating illiteracy, raising the living standards of the people. we know the situation from the political work too. in recent years, venezuela has held more democratic elections at all levels than any other european country. the opposition cannot accept democratic rules. the streets are led by the well-fed, children of wealthy families who lose their privileges. the european parliament must not consent to this attack on a sovereign people, it must not consent to the expansionist policy of the united states. we stand in solidarity with the people and government of venezuela who are defending their conquests and social rights. answer "blue card". mr. chairman, i think i've answered that. i do not know what country you are from, otherwise i could refer to your own experiences and to what the media in your country show. obviously there is violence, there are riots, there are clashes, there are dead. have you ever thought about what events are responsible for death? we all admit that there is violence. the question is where it starts, where it goes and how it is dealt with. and i have argued that venezuela is a democratic country and now it is under attack aimed at undermining the gains that have been made. the temptation is oil - several colleagues have admitted this - we have known this for so long. the european parliament must therefore defend a sovereign people who are willingly taking a course that some may not like.
mr president, it is a fact that many european countries have been affected by climate change in recent times - from spain, britain, portugal, to greece and italy. it is also a fact that climate change is evolving. we have a series of reports from international organizations -- from the intergovernmental panel on climate change report to, earlier, the world bank report and nicholas stern's report -- that show that prevention is the best way to protect both the climate and the economy and the well-being of citizens. we know what we have to do, but what is needed is the political will to achieve it. the commission is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environmentally friendly use of energy. on the other hand, we have a number of instruments, such as the european solidarity fund, for disasters caused by climate change. the resources are limited and must therefore be invested in the right way. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. the ecological principle must also be applied to compensate for the damage in a climate-sensitive way.
in writing. first of all, i would like to express my solidarity with the citizens affected by the adverse weather conditions. prevention in the field of natural disaster response is of paramount importance as it is less costly than responding to natural disasters. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, including the establishment of a european environment agency, the establishment of a european environment agency and the establishment of a european environment agency. it should also be made clear that local and regional authorities play a vital role in the disaster management cycle and must be actively involved in the design and implementation of disaster risk reduction and prevention strategies. finally, i cannot fail to mention the ongoing negotiations on the reform of the eu solidarity fund. we are aiming to complete the negotiations in the current parliamentary term to make the fund more efficient and flexible. in this connection, i support the introduction of a criterion for regional disasters, but with a minimum activation threshold of 1% of regional gdp. i also welcome the introduction of the possibility of making advances at the request of the affected country to deal more quickly and immediately with the consequences of disasters.

in writing. the report's findings on prostitution, exploitation and violence against women are devoid of any substance and are a hypocritical evasion of the social causes of the phenomenon, which the report is trying to hide. prostitution is a phenomenon inherent in exploitative societies, it is the ultimate form of exploitation and commodification of man himself. the report's position that trafficking in human beings is increasing 'due to the growth of organised crime' obscures the real causes of the phenomenon. the anti-people policy of the eu and the bourgeois governments is generating and magnifying poverty, unemployment, commodifying health, welfare, education. the false separation of allegedly 'voluntary' and 'coercive' exploits coercion to justify 'legal' prostitution. the 'scandinavian model' presented as a solution is a management option which neither wants nor can eliminate the phenomenon, since it leaves intact the causes that give rise to it and multiply it. it is the exploitative capitalist system that undermines the value of man, that drives young women into prostitution by the hand of hunger and the corruption of conscience. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the rules on the protection of the environment are respected.
in writing. the european investigation warrant directive promotes the strategic planning of the eu and its member state governments to harmonise criminal rules and criminal procedures, in a common framework of eu criminal rules, which, together with the giant web of eu and national repression mechanisms, chains even these civil rights to the most reactionary limits imposed by the principles and values of the alliance of capital, the european union. the criminal harmonisation of the rules in the eu is gradually seeking to achieve its ultimate goal of stifling restriction, the abolition of democratic rights and popular freedoms, the exercise of the dictatorship of monopolies, without restrictions and pretexts.

in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund for spain to support 560 redundant workers in 198 textile enterprises in the valencia region for the sum of eur 840 000. the continuing economic crisis in spain further highlights the added value of the european globalisation adjustment fund as a tool of european union social policy. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. moreover, the support provided is the practical expression of the union's solidarity. the spanish regions are subject to a process of rationalization and drastic cuts. the strengthening of the european globalisation adjustment fund will be able to contribute to the fight against unemployment without significantly burdening the efforts of fiscal consolidation of spain's national and regional budgets.

in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the granting of an ee guarantee to the european investment bank to cover losses from financing activities supporting investment projects outside the union for the period 2014-2020. i would like to thank the rapporteur, mr ivailo kalfin, who has proposed raising the overall ceiling of the european investment bank's financing activities under the eu guarantee from the commission's original proposal of eur 28 million to eur 30 million. the rapporteur's proposal not only succeeded in establishing the position of the whole of the european community, but in the interinstitutional negotiations which followed it was also accepted by the council. parliament also reached agreement with the council on important issues such as the regional ceilings for the bank's financing activities, greater consideration of environmental standards in the allocation of finance and the need to assess the impact of finance on the development of the country receiving it. by overriding this compromise text, the bank is enabled, under the cover of the eu budget guarantee, to finance projects outside the eu in order to support the union's external action and promote its role as a major global player.

rapporteur. - madam president, may i also start by thanking everybody for having worked so hard in the past two years on this dossier. without this incredible commitment by everybody, we would not have been able to be here today and voting on the data protection package in plenary tomorrow, thus sending to european citizens the message that the european parliament is aware of their concerns, that we are taking them very seriously, and that the european parliament is working for the interests of european citizens. may i also thank the commission and commissioner reding and her entire team for their commitment, work and good cooperation. we enjoyed working on this file. my appreciation goes also to the greek presidency. i know and recognise the effort that the presidency is putting into this file, but the presidency will also allow me to express my dissatisfaction and frustration about the fact that it is the council, and at least some member states, which are the reasons why we will not be able to reach the goal that we had set, namely to have the data protection reform package passed by the end of this parliament’s mandate. i doubt that the governments concerned will be able to explain this as necessary to citizens in their respective countries, particularly in view of the upcoming elections to the european parliament. parliament has been calling for years for a comprehensive single instrument on data protection that would cover both the private and the public sectors, including the police and judicial cooperation in criminal matters. this is why this parliament has always, right from the beginning, regarded the proposed regulation and directive as a package: what we call the ‘package approach’, and all our legislative work on the data protection reform has been conducted on precisely this premise. let me remind everybody that there was widespread agreement on this even before the two legislative proposals were issued. it was the report by axel voss back in 2010 which stressed the idea of a comprehensive and consistent approach, also covering processing in the areas of police and judicial cooperation, including processing at domestic level. as a consequence, we followed parallel procedures in our work as regards the timing and content of the regulation and directive, and both i and jan albrecht – and also the commission, as repeatedly stated by commissioner reding – confirmed this commitment to the package approach several times. i do not need to elaborate on the regulation. jan albrecht has already presented the necessary facts, which i fully endorse, and i would like to thank jan once again for all the work and commitment he and his team have put into this file. the directive itself, if approved tomorrow, will bring significant improvements to the processing of personal data by police and judicial authorities in criminal matters. but let me be very frank and very direct. it has come to my knowledge that a number of colleagues have decided to vote against the directive tomorrow. colleagues, how exactly will you justify to your voters that you choose to protect them against, for example, google, but not against police and judicial authorities that tend to act arbitrarily? how can we explain to european citizens that we will vote in favour of the nsa report tomorrow, which reproaches a third country for the hidden actions of its secret services, while we, as european union member states, refuse to comply with similar rules? and how will you explain, in the event that the directive is not adopted by this parliament tomorrow, that it will be the european parliament itself that has abandoned the package approach and that we have killed the directive, doing the dirty work that the governments of some member states would so much like to do? i shall finish with an appeal to colleagues to do exactly that: to show european citizens that we are taking their fundamental rights seriously by voting in favour of both legislative instruments tomorrow so that they can be negotiated with the european council as a whole during the next legislature.
rapporteur. - madam president, may i also thank my colleagues for their comments, their good words and also their support. i have also noticed with some satisfaction that all interventions today have reaffirmed the package approach and its importance. may i express the hope that colleagues will also confirm this with the vote tomorrow? after all, it is about our credibility: the credibility of the european parliament vis-à-vis the european citizen. so may i once again appeal to colleagues to vote tomorrow in favour of both reports, the regulation and the directive. of course i will follow what some colleagues have said, for instance sophie in ‘t veld or carlos coelho. the package presented is not perfect, this is clear. but it is the right step in the right direction, and we should take this into consideration. most of all it will be a necessary and important message to our citizens that we are taking their concerns very much into consideration, especially in such difficult times – sensitive times – after the nsa scandal and all the other revelations. but our vote tomorrow will also send the necessary message to the council and to member states to speed up their preparations and come to the trilogue, so that we can finalise this package and present to our citizens a real and effective reform of the data protection package.
answer to a question on the 'blue card' procedure to axel voss. mr voss, i must confess i am very, very surprised by this statement you have made. after two years of joint work on this package of regulations and directives, it is a little bold to say that the wishes of a group in this house have not been taken into account, if i may say so openly, especially when this group has not put any proposals on the table in the area of the directive, but has merely pursued a delaying tactic, similar to the delaying tactics that some governments of some member states have shown here.
mr president, may i join in first of all in thanking the rapporteur, claude moraes, and the shadow rapporteurs for their excellent work and in expressing my deep disappointment about the fact that the european union, the council and the member states have not reacted at all on the nsa revelations. as a european citizen, i feel offended and abandoned. there is maybe one positive aspect regarding the nsa scandal: it was a real wake-up call regarding data protection reform in the european union. i will never forget the words that the german chancellor, angela merkel, used during the german electoral campaign a few months ago. she said, when she was asked about the nsa scandal, that the only answer to the nsa scandal is more data protection at european level and fast, as soon as possible. i hope that certain political groups in the house will follow the words of their most prominent representative.
