to convince the european citizens that the union is useful to its citizens in both small and large matters. the single european air transport market over the last 20 years has made flying affordable for millions of new passengers. but, the increasing traffic puts enormous pressure on air traffic management. and as traffic escalates, we'll have more and more delays, fewer and more expensive flights, and questions about flight safety. air traffic services are not prepared for the 21st century and the combination of safe and cheap flights, without delays, has, unfortunately, an expiration date. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the air traffic control system is properly functioning. a network of stereotypes, of prejudices, but also of interests. the present situation of 38 different and incompatible air navigation systems and 60 different control centres is unacceptably harmful to the passenger and to the companies in the union. and of course this has nothing to do with whether the service provider is public or private. the u.s. provides them publicly, just like we do, but with one provider instead of 38 and, ultimately, at half the cost. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's airports are able to compete with those of the east. the changes needed are organisational and not about staff competence and ability. it is an achievement of air traffic services that the great increase in traffic over the last 20 years has been achieved with the highest safety guarantee we know and of course require when flying in europe. but to take the next step we must move on to rationalization, that is to say finally to the realization of the single european sky. the commission has already taken the initiative in the area of the environment, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support the amendments tabled by the committee on the environment, public health and consumer protection. in writing. the report of the committee on economic and monetary affairs on the role of the troika was 'legislative'. the report legitimizes the role and functioning of the troika and is incompatible with the tragic reality in greece. the troika model and the imf involvement are presented as a one-way street. the lack of democratic accountability and institutional legitimacy is justified by the report's tight timeframe and the urgency of the situation. the harsh austerity measures imposed on the countries under the memorandum, which have led to a humanitarian crisis, are also justified as necessary. the report also argues that if these programmes had not been implemented, the consequences would have been worse. in the same context, the cercas report, which makes some references to social problems, essentially treats the increase in unemployment, poverty and insecurity as phenomena and not as direct disastrous consequences of the neoliberal policies implemented in these countries. finally, neither report calls for the countries to be immediately disengaged from the memoranda and the troika. the commission's proposal is to be implemented by the member states, and the commission's proposal is to be implemented by the member states. i have voted against both reports for all the above reasons.

in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the effectiveness of the european social fund's expenditure on older workers. firstly, it is worrying that the definition of 'older workers' is not consistently used in the operational programmes for the period 2007-13. the weaknesses in the implementation of the european social fund are also a cause for concern, particularly as regards the lack of data from the member states to assess whether the resources have been used correctly and whether the actions have been effective in meeting the needs of older workers. in the current 2014-2020 programming period, it is therefore imperative, firstly, to establish specific indicators to make the link between the actions undertaken and the objectives set clear, secondly, to implement a better monitoring system to correct the programmes in case of non-fulfilment of the objectives set and, thirdly, to establish an effective data collection system for older workers.

in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the granting of a discharge for the 2012 financial year to the artemis joint undertaking, the main objective of which is to define and implement a 'research agenda' for the development of key technologies for embedded computing systems in various fields of application, in order to enhance european competitiveness and sustainability and to enable the emergence of new markets and applications in society. i would like to express my concern that, for the second year in a row, the european court of auditors has issued a qualified opinion on the legality and regularity of the transactions on which the annual accounts are based. the joint undertaking was not able to assess whether the ex post control strategy provides sufficient assurance as to the legality and regularity of the underlying transactions. therefore, in cooperation with the european court of auditors, artemis should without delay improve the quality of its ex ante and ex post controls in order to improve this situation in the coming years. the european parliament will await with interest the outcome of these processes.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the discharge for the 2012 financial year to the clean sky joint undertaking, the purpose of which is to accelerate the development, validation and promotion of clean technologies in the field of air transport in the european union, with a view to their exploitation as soon as possible. i would like to welcome the progress made by this joint undertaking, as for 2012, the european court of auditors delivered an unqualified opinion on its annual accounts as regards the legality and regularity of the underlying transactions, whereas for 2011 the same institution had expressed reservations. this progress is significant and should be continued, as there is room for further improvement in areas such as budget implementation, strengthening of internal control systems and, in particular, the validation of the control certificates accompanying beneficiaries' declarations of expenditure.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the discharge for the financial year 2012 of the eniac joint undertaking for the implementation of the joint technology initiatives for nanoelectronics, the main objective of which is to define and implement a research agenda for the development of key capabilities in the field of nanoelectronics in various fields of application. i would like to express my concern that, for the second year in a row, the european court of auditors has issued a qualified opinion on the legality and regularity of the transactions on which the annual accounts are based. the court was not able to assess whether the ex post control strategy, which relies heavily on the national funding authorities to verify the declarations of expenditure for projects, provides sufficient assurance as to the legality and regularity of the transactions underlying the accounts. however, the joint undertaking has adopted an ex post audit strategy for 2012, the implementation of which has already started. it is therefore necessary for eniac to report to parliament on the results of the above strategy, accompanied by an evaluation by the european court of auditors.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the granting of a discharge for the 2012 financial year to the fuel cells and hydrogen joint undertaking aimed at supporting, in a coordinated manner, research and technological development activities with a view to focusing on the development of commercial applications to facilitate further industry efforts to rapidly deploy fuel cell and hydrogen technologies. i would like to express my satisfaction that the joint undertaking is not facing any major problems in its budgetary and financial management. however, there is scope for further improvements in cash management and internal control systems. finally, i would like to express my support for the improvements proposed by the european court of auditors to the monitoring and reporting system covering the protection, dissemination and transfer of the results of the research carried out by the agency.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the discharge to be given to the joint undertaking for the implementation of the joint technology initiative on innovative medicines (imi joint undertaking) for the financial year 2012. the joint undertaking aims to significantly improve the efficiency and effectiveness of the drug development process, with the long-term aim of enabling the pharmaceutical industry to produce more effective and safer innovative medicines. it is of concern that for 2012, the ex post audit error rate was 5,82%, and that the european court of auditors delivered a qualified opinion on the legality and regularity of the transactions underlying the joint undertaking's annual accounts for the second consecutive year. however, it should be recognised that most of the abovementioned errors were in relatively small amounts (below eur 5[nbsp ]000) which will be adjusted in favour of the joint undertaking. in addition, measures have since been taken to recover or offset these amounts from subsequent requests for reimbursement of expenditure, and the rate will continue to evolve on a multiannual basis as more projects, beneficiaries and requests are audited.
in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the granting of a discharge for the financial year 2012 to the iter joint undertaking, the main tasks of which are: (i) to provide the euratom contribution to the international energy agency for iter fusion (ii) providing the contribution of euratom to the 'broader vision activities' (supplementary joint fusion research activities) with japan for the rapid realisation of fusion energy and (iii) developing and coordinating a programme of activities to prepare for the construction of a fusion reactor and associated facilities, including the international fusion irradiation facility. there is scope for further improvement in financial management, in particular in terms of high cash balances, internal control systems and procedures for awarding operating contracts and grants. the joint undertaking shall provide the commission with the necessary information to enable it to assess the impact of the union contribution on the union's energy performance. the committee also welcomed the commission's proposal to extend the scope of the programme to include the development of new technologies, and called for the commission to take the necessary steps to ensure that the programme is implemented in a way that is compatible with the objectives of the programme.

in writing. this is an important proposal in the field of road transport aimed at revising the directive on maximum weights and dimensions of road vehicles. the purpose of the text we are adopting is to allow more aerodynamic vehicles with better energy efficiency to be introduced on the market, thus contributing to fuel savings and reducing emissions. at the same time, it aims to improve road safety, with significant benefits for vulnerable road users such as pedestrians and cyclists. in addition, the extension of the vehicles will facilitate the reloading of modern wheeled containers, either through fleet renewal or by necessary conversions. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is implemented in a uniform manner, and i would like to ask the council to take the necessary steps to ensure that the directive is implemented in a uniform manner.
in writing. we all recognise that the outcome of the mfa negotiations was disappointing and fell short of the eu's ambitions and political priorities. the council's position during the negotiations was also at least problematic and, in order to avoid the negotiations on the current mff being seen as precedent, it should be ensured that the european council's conclusions do not constitute a red line which cannot be negotiated with parliament. as regards the next steps, it is important to ensure during the current period the full implementation of the various flexibility mechanisms successfully negotiated by parliament and included in the mff regulation; it is also important to ensure that the approval of the new president of the commission is conditional on a strong political commitment to implement the mandatory post-implementation review of the mff. in addition, the report of the high level group on own resources provides a unique opportunity to overcome the impasse that has arisen in the financing of the union budget. finally, in the long term, the question of the duration of the mff should be considered in order to better coordinate it with the mandate of the european parliament and the commission.
madam president, six years ago we saw the collapse of the financial system, the casino that created surpluses for the many and the powerful. a system that has received guarantees and other gifts from the european union states - some 4 trillion euros - at a time when the unemployed in europe are 26 million. we have seen the protectors of this system, the mafia of speculators, the rating agencies, hammer greece into a debt crisis and into the memoranda, and then attack ireland, portugal, spain, italy and finally cyprus. we have seen, ladies and gentlemen, four years ago how the european union, mrs merkel, mr sarkozy, mr barroso, sacrificed greece to save the european banks, as mr olli rehn admitted to us with great cynicism. the banking union envisaged by the council, the commission, the european central bank and, unfortunately, the majority of parliament today, is a show of mercy to the banking system, which bears a huge responsibility for the economic situation in europe today. instead of creating opportunities for recovery and growth for european societies, the planned banking union is strengthening the zombie banks, strengthening a financial system which will continue to generate 'bubbles', imbalances and crises.
it is very important, madam president, and symbolically very significant, that in this last plenary session we are ratifying the agreement on the second pillar of banking union, the single bank resolution mechanism and the single resolution fund, which, together with the single supervisory mechanism, lay the foundations for the removal of the fragmentation and dangerous imbalances in europe's banking system. sound banks with uniform and transparent rules on supervision, operation and resolution, when and if needed, not only protect taxpayers from burdens beyond their reach, but also represent a decisive step towards restoring liquidity and financing to the real economy. all of this is very important and i am pleased that the greek presidency has made a major contribution to reaching an agreement, and i am particularly pleased, mr president-in-office, that you have rightly pointed out that the banking union also has a third pillar which must not be abandoned: the launch of the single european deposit guarantee scheme with a single fund modelled on the single clearing fund. i hope, commissioner barnier - and i thank you for your excellent work in general and in particular on banking union - that the next european commission will commit itself to moving forward and implementing this third pillar which is absolutely essential for banking union.
madam president, it is good that there is a minimum level of guarantees for small and medium-sized depositors at european union level and that the provision for uncertain claims by banks is increased. i consider the effort a success, although i fear that the effects of the economic crisis are again being passed on to the taxpayers, that is to say, to the depositors, and not to the shareholders, and especially not to the supervisory bodies which have responsibilities. for five years now, we have pretended to ignore the fact that the crisis is also a monetary one. the european central bank needs to take additional monetary easing measures to address the low inflation and the strong euro which are holding back the growth of the economy and the competitiveness of the majority of member states. i think we must restore the euro to the dollar, which was overturned after the issuance of the six trillion dollars by the united states of america. i would like to take this opportunity to thank all my colleagues, and especially commissioner barnier, for the efforts he has made in his portfolio and for the cooperation we have had so far.

in writing. the vote in the european parliament today on the banking union by the parties of capital and the euro-trend is a compromise in the eurozone and the eu that will accelerate the concentration and centralisation of capital in the financial system with painful consequences for the working class and the popular strata. the banking union in the eu serves the objectives of ensuring the profitability of monopoly groups and shielding their competitiveness by strengthening the banking system of the eu member states, creating stronger and more stable banks, concentrating and centralising capital and at the same time shifting the burdens of the crisis and the depreciation of capital onto the backs of the people, either as retail depositors or as taxpayers. the call for more favourable treatment of the lower classes as depositors or as borrowers is false and misleading. banks in capitalism invest their capital in order to make a profit, not to satisfy the needs of the people. the banking union is added to the anti-people framework of 'enhanced economic governance', of the eu's permanent memoranda for all its member states against the peoples and the strengthening of the monopoly giants. it is another link in the chains of anti-people measures and commitments imposed by the eu and urban governments on workers to strengthen the monopoly behemoths.

in writing. i voted in favour of the report on the mobilisation of the european globalisation adjustment fund for spain to support 185 redundancies in the santana group and its suppliers and downstream users in the automotive sector. the continuing economic crisis in spain further highlights the added value of the european globalisation adjustment fund as a tool of the european union's social policy. the targeted financial support it provides for programmes for the retraining and reintegration of workers affected by collective redundancies is of particular importance. moreover, the support provided is the practical expression of the union's solidarity. the spanish regions are subject to a process of rationalization and drastic cuts. the strengthening of the european globalisation adjustment fund will be able to contribute to the fight against unemployment without significantly burdening the efforts of fiscal consolidation of spain's national and regional budgets.
in writing. the 'securities investment enterprises' directive and the related european parliament report (a7-0125/2013) have as their main objective to further facilitate the free movement of capital in the eu single capital market. the whole eu legislative package voted by the european parliament at its last plenary session before the european elections is aimed at strengthening the stock market, in particular the market for derivatives and "products" on the stock market, the most aggressive speculative part of capital. the european institutions and the political staff of capital seek to concentrate the monopoly financial groups operating in the sector, to ensure their profitability and to strengthen their position in the inter-imperialist competitions that are raging. the exploitation of the working class and the popular strata will intensify to increase corporate profits. the bourgeois and opportunist arguments about alleged market regulation and the restriction of speculation are an attempt to manipulate and disorient the people. they hide the fact that the capitalist crisis is the result of capitalist development, the over-accumulation of capital that cannot find a profitable outlet. they point to some highly paid business executives and some "irresponsible speculators" as the ones allegedly responsible in order to exonerate the capitalist system that generates crises with its insurmountable contradictions and contradictions. that is why it is not regulated, nor humanized - it is only reversed.

in writing. i voted in favour of the report because ecall will be based on the installation of type-approved equipment for the single european emergency call number 112, starting with certain vehicle categories, in all vehicles and will also create a framework for handling ecall calls from telecommunications networks and psaps. this approach ensures that ecall is made available as an eu-wide service to all citizens in europe, accelerates uptake and unlocks the full potential of ecall in order to limit loss of life and to respond quickly to serious injuries. the manufacturer shall ensure that the 112-based ecall in-vehicle system is designed and manufactured in such a way that it can be easily and quickly connected to the network.

in writing. - the european parliament adopted the framework governing the selection process and the course of the european capitals of culture from 2020 to 2033. the present initiative, which i voted in favour of, is the product of months of negotiations between the european parliament and the european council. the first european capital of culture was athens in 1985, and the main event of the institution, which provides for significant financial support for cities, is the "melina mercouri" prize. the new action plan highlights the role of local communities, citizen participation and the international role further enhanced by the diaspora. more flexible funding will now be provided, in line with the conditions, to facilitate each city's preparation without compromising on transparency, monitoring and evaluation. under the new framework, one of the cultural capitals of 2021 will be a greek city, which is very important, considering that the development opportunities opened up in cities and surrounding areas are boosting the local economy for much longer than a year and that the cultural capitals are ensuring a visibility that significantly enhances their tourism product and modernise their cultural infrastructure, among many other benefits.
in writing. the kke voted against the report and the commission's proposal for a directive because it is another anti-working class piece of equipment added to the barrage of anti-people measures unleashed by the eu institutions and the bourgeois governments against the working people. the new directive undermines and effectively nullifies even this minimal protection of the most basic wage and employment rights of those workers employed in another eu member state than the one in which the company is established. it also removes any minimal restrictions on the tendency of companies to choose as their home member states those with crumbling wages and labour rights and then apply those miserable working and wage conditions to workers employed in all the other eu member states. the workers become prey to the capitalists, who, using slave-trading offices and various "subcontractors", relieve them of any obligation, even that of paying their hard-earned wages, and leave the unpaid workers to chase after "ghost" companies. all this means the salary action of bulgaria, romania, poland, estonia, latvia or rather china and india, as provided for in the "euro pact", the abolition of the sces, the striking of the right to strike everywhere, in all eu member states, to save and increase the profits of the monopoly groups.
in writing. we consider the problem of returning all kinds of cultural goods, which are the expression of the artistic creation of each place and reflect the historical path and cultural character of each people, to be of the highest priority. in this sense, cultural goods belong to the people who created them and are an integral part of the natural environment in which they were created. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the directive is implemented in a uniform manner. in particular, the directive refers only to 'illegally exported' cultural goods and only to those seized after 1993. in order to provide a substantive solution to the problem, the following principles should be established: the purchase, sale and possession by a private individual or by any private or public body of a member state of any cultural object originating in another state (eu or non-eu) is considered illegal and prohibited. these cultural goods are returned to the country of origin automatically and without any special procedure or claim for compensation. the commission's proposal, which is a step backwards from the 1993 milestone, tackles the problem of the immense archaeological treasures which were taken from our country during the german occupation and which must finally be returned.
in writing. - the legislative initiative in question, which i supported, is a technical and very important text for the safeguarding of cultural property, both in practice and in terms of concepts. this is a legislative framework which recognises the dual concept of cultural heritage, both as a good and as a product. at the same time, it sets the conditions for strengthening control without damaging the national cultural heritage of the eu member states, which is also a cultural asset of the union itself. this initiative strengthens legal barriers but imposes additional conditions against international organised crime in cases of theft and trafficking of cultural works and goods. this text refers to the 1993 council and european parliament directive on the return of cultural objects unlawfully removed from the territory of a member state, which sought to ensure the protection of cultural objects and in particular national treasures after the abolition of controls at the internal borders of the eu on 1 january 1993. it cannot therefore cover cases such as the return of the parthenon marbles but it can at least help to strengthen the dialogue in this direction, as indeed in any similar case of other eu member states.
in writing. i voted in favour of mrs vergiat's report because it aims to simplify the procedures for the recovery of works of art or other objects considered to be 'national treasures' which have been illegally removed from the territory of a member state since 1993. it is noted that several eu countries have suffered serious theft and illegal export of cultural goods since the creation of the single market.
in writing. - the mediterranean has played a significant role in international and regional geopolitical and economic developments throughout history, as determined by conflicts and events that continue to make the region unstable. hundreds of thousands of people try to enter the eu each year from its southern sea borders, namely the sea area of greece, italy, spain, malta and cyprus. these states, hit hard by the economic crisis of recent years, were suddenly faced with the problem of a large number of displaced persons seeking refuge in safer territories. this proposal, which i supported, sets the conditions for increased effectiveness of migration policy and border management and surveillance between member states and third countries, and for improved cooperation between eu bodies such as frontex in the context of the maritime operations it coordinates. finally, this initiative once again highlights the need for an integrated european migration policy, based on solidarity and fair sharing of responsibilities between member states, in accordance with international law and human rights provisions.

investment files were also concluded while progress was made in the fields of telecommunications and infrastructure. better funding, more market flexibility, more access to the markets for everyone, more access to liquidity for smes, more transparency across our union. all these are different aspects of two things: more integration and more competitiveness. in other words, better prospects for job creation, employment, growth and prosperity, especially at times when huge unemployment levels threaten the fragile social cohesion in many of our member states. thirdly, border and migration management: this presents major challenges not only for the european south, but indeed for the whole of the eu, with far-reaching repercussions on economic growth and social cohesion. important achievements here were the revision of the list of countries for which a visa was required to enter the eu allowing citizens of some 20 non-eu countries to travel to the eu without a visa and the adoption of the directive on the conditions of entry and stay of third-country nationals in the context of intra-corporate transfers as well as, of course, the new frontex regulation. a major breakthrough was the adoption of the post-stockholm strategic guidelines in the area of freedom, security and justice which focus attention on the implementation of the principle of solidarity in the areas of asylum, border and migration management, the link between migration and the union's foreign policy that is cooperation with third countries and the more for more principle, etc. the development of effective return, readmission and visa policies, and continuous attention to an effective internal security framework. fourthly, the horizontal theme of the hellenic presidency has been to redefine and re-launch eu maritime policies in all their aspects, including security, growth and energy. a key component is the european maritime security strategy. this is the first time that the eu has developed such a holistic, cross-sectoral strategy, which will result in better protecting and promoting the security and economic interests of the eu and its member states at sea. it defines strategic interests and threats in the global maritime domain. the strategy promotes eu principles and values, the rule of law, democracy, respect for human rights, energy efficiency and security and a rules-based approach to maritime security, as reflected in the un international law of the sea. at institutional level, the adoption of the regulation on the statute and funding of european political parties and foundations is also a qualitative leap for european representative democracy, through genuinely european political parties. i congratulate parliament for voting this through. particular attention was also paid to enlargement, thus renewing relevant eu interest, while confirming the engagement of those countries to fulfill their accession criteria. a week ago, albania was awarded candidate status, while five months ago accession negotiations started with serbia. as regards turkey, it has been underlined in the framework of the eu-turkey association council that turkey's eu path depends on its fulfilling its eu obligations relating to cyprus. in all, the greek presidency concluded 67 pieces of legislation and advanced another 15 legislative initiatives, where consensus was reached between the commission and the council. this was done in four months, instead of the usual duration of six months, because of the european election. and we did it with a minimal budget of which not more than 40% was finally spent! now, we are particularly happy that the next presidency, the italian presidency, will take the lead and push all policies and initiatives further. we are very hopeful and optimistic that it will exceed all expectations so that 2014 will be regarded by everyone as a very successful, mediterranean year; a proud year for every european citizen! in the last three years, my country has been seriously challenged. europe was also challenged. some were predicting the so-called grexit, the idea that greece should exit the euro. some were predicting that the euro itself would not make it either. we proved them wrong. europe worked. our union has problems, but it also has the capacity to solve those problems and go ahead. we have proved that our peoples across our union can work together and build their common future in times of prosperity, obviously, but also during trying and very difficult moments of crisis. we proved that people across our union can change their countries in making them fitter and more competitive so that they can successfully demand change in our union, to make it stronger, safer, with more competitive economies and more stable democracies. in the last european elections, the european peoples did not vote against europe. on the contrary, they voted for more europe and better europe! i believe we have already started delivering although we still have a long way to go. but we have to do more than that. we have to bring our union closer to the hearts of the europeans. to prove that our union is not a series of bureaucratic procedures that nobody can follow, and that it is mainly about common prospects and common ideals that everyone shares and can understand right away. just a hundred years ago, a devastating war started here. it wasn't the only one in the years to come? it was dreadful, but not the worst one, either. our peoples suffered again and again one tragedy after another for decades. now europe is the most united we have ever had. the most democratic we have ever had. the most prosperous and most stable we've ever had in centuries. yet our people need to be inspired by our union and by its prospects. they need to feel our union closer to their hearts. i believe this is your first and foremost priority here in the european parliament: to bring our union closer to the hearts of our peoples! because you, in our european parliament, are indeed the very heart of our union.
president of the commission. - mr president, the end of the hellenic presidency marks the end of one parliamentary term and the beginning of a new one so it took place in a very specific situation. also, these last months have coincided with the beginning of the exit from the deep crisis in the european union, perhaps the most difficult we have had since the creation of the european community in the 1950s. we have now started on the road to recovery, but there is still a long way to go. i think it is important that, precisely at this moment, a country like greece, one of those most affected by the crisis, has been able – showing the european commitment of its leadership – not only to respond to the challenges at home but also to contribute constructively to our european agenda. i would like to thank personally prime minister samaras for the way in which he and his competent and dedicated team have carried out their duties and responsibilities, leading the council during this very specific and challenging time. under his skilful captaincy, the hellenic presidency has worked extremely hard – across many issues – to put in place what was necessary and to bring important decisions to fruition, against extremely tight deadlines because of the european parliament elections. prime minister samaras has already mentioned many of these issues so i will not list them again. i would prefer to concentrate my attention on the big achievements in relation to the economic situation. the reality is that economic confidence has improved and is improving in europe. growth across the european union is predicted at 1.6% this year, rising to 2% by 2015. investment is rebounding. our collective excessive deficits are now under the 3% mark for the first time since the beginning of the crisis. six more member states have now exited the excessive deficit procedure, bringing the total number down to 11, fewer than half of the 24 countries which were in this position in 2011. i believe this is progress; and it shows that the stability and growth pact is working. during the last months, and following ireland’s successful exit from the programme and the successful implementation of the programme in spain for the banks, portugal too has exited its financial assistance programme with success. the situation in greece and cyprus has stabilised, with real signs of improvement, particularly in terms of the correction of very important imbalances. we all know this process has been painful. i would say that prime minister samaras and his government know it better than anybody. however, after these difficult and necessary reforms, greece has now seen a further reduction in its deficit; it has projected growth; it has corrected historic external imbalances; and, indeed, it is recovering its competitiveness. despite the many prophets of doom, greece has remained an integral, loyal, member of the euro area. i remember that, not so long ago, i personally had to discuss these matters not only with many in the european union, but also outside the union with those predicting a greek exit and those predicting the implosion of the euro and even the disintegration of the european union. it would be helpful if they could consider that they were wrong. nobody denies that the recovery is still fragile and uneven. growth is modest. unemployment, particularly youth unemployment, is, the biggest challenge facing us. we cannot afford a jobless recovery or a lost generation. that is why we need to speed up the work that is now on the table of the council and parliament. in fact, it is now possible to make more progress in that direction because very important achievements have been realised during these last months. some of these developments were considered unthinkable a few years ago. if you had asked analysts in europe and outside europe, just two or three years ago, whether it would be possible to have a banking union and to have european central bank competence over national banks, they would have said it was not realistic. i remember, when the commission proposed the banking union in 2012, that when i used the words ‘banking union’ many people said this was not realistic. they did not even accept the words. they said: ‘ok, something about financial stability, but do not talk about banking union’ – and now we have a banking union. we now have agreement on both the single supervisory mechanism and the single resolution mechanism and fund. this was a crucial sign of our commitment. it was a very important sign to the markets, and to our citizens, that economic and monetary union is here to stay and is going to be completed. alongside other important pieces of financial markets regulation, this will strengthen confidence and stability in the financial markets, and it will help to restore lending to the economy. let us be honest: without stability any prospect of growth is impossible. if this work of stability had not been done we could not now consider, as we are considering, the ways to foster growth at european level. another important issue is the european semester. it is already in its fourth edition, it has now reached cruising speed. it has taken us further down the road of economic coordination, which is also a prerequisite given the high level of interdependence of our economies. on the country-specific recommendations agreed at the european council last week, i want to underline that there was unanimous agreement, because sometimes i have the impression that those who agree at the european council, or at council level, then forget what they have agreed and say it is the others who have agreed. i repeat: the endorsement of the country-specific recommendations was unanimous in the member states, so it combines all the governments, left, right, north and south. this exercise is now making real progress. we are now in a phase of moving member states from crisis mode to a more forward-looking, reform-oriented perspective. the european semester, too, was unimaginable a few years ago. if, some years ago, we had asked whether the commission would one day have the power to look at the national budgets before they are adopted, people would have said it was impossible – and now it is not only possible, it is an obligation. why? because the crisis has highlighted how interdependent we are. the european semester, unimaginable a few years ago, has become an established focal point in the european calendar and economic governance. we now have in place a genuine debate about national economic policy, about what is happening in each of the member states, and i want to congratulate this house, the member states and the national parliaments, for the constructive role they have played, against very tight deadlines, in securing agreement on the country-specific recommendations and completing this semester of economic governance. that said – and i want also to highlight this – the idea being popularised in some countries that these are impositions from brussels, or from the commission, is completely false. that is not at all the way it works. the commission has the role of making proposals and is the initiator of the process. it is also the objective referee. the role of the commission is to check whether recommendations have been implemented or not at national level. but the decisions, i repeat, have been taken by all the governments, by the finance ministers and, ultimately, by the prime ministers of all our countries. so, please, it is time to avoid these discussions putting the blame on brussels for decisions that are taken by the governments themselves. i hope they will have the courage to stand by decisions they take collectively. we now need to focus specifically on implementation and there is no room for complacency because a lot of reforms are needed. we need to do more, for example, regarding employment, the youth guarantee, the fight against poverty and social exclusion, investment, taxation and, overall, on progress in terms of our competitiveness. these two developments – the european semester process and the completion of the banking union – were among the most important during the last six months. however, there are many other areas where we need to speed up our work, and i believe that the italian presidency has a lot of work to do here in terms of more focused action regarding the fight against youth unemployment and making better use of our eur 1 trillion budget for the next seven years. we hear talk of the ‘new marshall plan for europe’, but what we have is much more than the marshall plan. we have a budget for the next seven years which, if properly managed, can help us very much in terms of the investment needs which some of our countries do not themselves have a sufficient margin to meet. efficiency is another essential consideration, and that it why i was very happy with the results of the last european council in relation to refit, the programme for regulatory fitness at european level. during the last six months, through our refit programme, we have further lightened the load of legislation that no longer serves any useful purpose. we have withdrawn 53 more proposals, making a total of 293 withdrawn since 2006. it is interesting that these figures never appear publicly. in the conventional wisdom today there is still talk of bureaucracy from brussels. let me tell you that if all the governments made the same effort that we are making at european level to reduce the administrative burden, we could have a much better environment for our citizens and for our smes. let us not forget that 80% of the european budget is administered by member states themselves, and one-third of the administrative burden linked to eu legislation comes from national implementation. member states can and must play their role too, creating a more growth-friendly environment by cutting red tape, and this is a collective responsibility of the commission, of this parliament and also of the council. we have discussed other important issues during this semester. i will not repeat what has been said already in relation to the important area of justice, freedom and security, and especially the issue of migration. finally, let me say a word about enlargement because this has been a period in which we commemorated 10 years since the big enlargement of 2004. let us not forget that at the beginning of 2004 – when, by the way, my first commission took office – there were only 15 member states. now there are 28. we have almost doubled the membership. it is one of the greatest achievements ever, in terms of european history, to have this reunited continent. that is another reason why 2014 should be remembered: it is 25 years since the iron curtain came down, 70 years after d day, 100 years since the first world war, and also, in may, we celebrated 10 years of the reunification of europe. a union of 15 has been transformed into one of 28 and is open for the future, bringing together rich and less rich, big and small, old and new, central and peripheral, north, south, east and west. those member states have grown stronger economically, politically and democratically. latvia, which will hold the presidency from the start of 2015, successfully joined the euro area at the start of the year. lithuania – a country whose wealth has increased from 35% of the eu gdp average in 1995 to 78% – will become the 19th member of the euro area at the start of 2015. so, dear members of parliament, how can people still be negative about the enlargement and reunification of europe? how can they fail to understand the great convergence machine, the great creator of opportunities that the european union has been: not only working for those new member states but also projecting stability and thus avoiding many ukraines around us. for ukraine illustrates what would have happened if we had not had enlargement at the right time. i firmly believe that europe should remain open, particularly to the western balkans. it would be a complete mistake for europe to suggest at this stage, because of current difficulties, the closure of our borders. we have to manage some specific challenges, yes: freedom of movement is a sacred principle. we should combat any kind of misuse or abuse of the rights of freedom of movement, but it would be a real mistake for europe to close its borders. i have seen just recently, visiting serbia and albania, the progress that those countries, along with others, are making, and it would irresponsible on our part not to sustain the hope for those countries, if they meet the necessary conditions democratically and economically, of joining the european union when they are ready. this has been a very important presidency in very difficult times. prime minister samaras and his team have demonstrated their commitment, capacity and experience – indeed, it was the fifth hellenic presidency – as well as their strong national traditions. i believe that those traditions, which were apparent in the course of this presidency, can best be described by three greek words that underline our european approach as we face the challenge of the 21st century. logos implies pressing ahead with what is rational and right for europe; pathos means listening to all sides and understanding the needs of our citizens, especially those who are suffering the most; and ethos, implies preserving our european values of freedom, justice, and solidarity. i thank the greek presidency and i thank you for your attention.

madam president, lithuania tried to join the euro area in 2006 but it will achieve this in january 2015. this delay has proved very productive for lithuania and the european union. the country has had adequate time to prepare itself in a better way to face the challenge and the european union has found new ways of managing the systemic risks. in my view, lithuania’s successful participation in the euro area will depend on five factors. firstly, on the quality of its preparation. lithuania is much better prepared than greece was 14 years ago. to give you an example, lithuania’s public debt is less than 40% of gdp, whereas in greece, my country, it was more than 100 %. secondly, lithuania seems to be in a position to use the period following the adoption of the euro to solve its particular economic problems and catch up with the most advanced members of the euro area in terms of competitiveness. thirdly, the euro area has developed a sophisticated control system and therefore the systemic risk has been greatly reduced. fourthly, lithuania has put its finances and its economy in order. it now needs more development opportunities and a better financed banking system. finally, lithuania has to cope with a somewhat unstable international environment but the most probable scenario is that there will not be a major crisis which could deny the country its successful participation in the euro area. i would just like to congratulate our lithuanian friends and assure them that the comparison with greece lacks any substance. in writing. - i am voting in favour of the resolution on youth employment. youth unemployment rates in the eu are at unprecedented levels. the eu and the member states should agree together on a comprehensive approach to combating youth unemployment. the statistics of recent years are more than disappointing. the figures speak for themselves, as 25% of young people of working age, or 5.5 million young people, are unemployed in europe, whereas in my country, greece, the figure is 60%. in greece, young people are migrating in search of work and livelihood - a phenomenon also observed in the countries of the south which are in economic crisis. we can't afford to be idle. we need responsible and targeted policies that deliver tangible results: - by speeding up the implementation of the youth employment initiative so that eligible member states receive the funds as soon as possible, - by creating sustainable and high-quality jobs, and - by developing an action plan on youth employment and labour market choice and decent income. in an ageing europe, our young people are the literature of europe. so let's get them off the sidelines!
in writing. the kke votes against the joint resolution and at the same time rejects all the draft resolutions tabled in the plenary on youth employment because, despite their individual differences, they are identical in their strategy, accept the anti-people eu strategy europe 2020, put forward as the cause of the situation one or the other mix of management of the capitalist development path, each of them declaring its support for it. they support the subsidization of employers, beyond hot money, with tax breaks and contributions, with free work, subsidizing their profitability in the name of competitiveness. their aim is to drastically reduce the price of labour as a means of overcoming the crisis in favour of capital and at the expense of the working class and the young. in other words, any capitalist recovery will be based on the demolition of all the workers' conquests. this policy has not only failed to tackle youth unemployment, but has worsened their situation, reduced the general level of wages, generalised flexible forms of employment and further exacerbated the exploitation of workers and young people.

madam president, in a european union where many member states are suffering from the economic crisis, the imposition of an embargo by russia, mainly on agricultural products but also on other products, is the tip of the iceberg. as greek citizens, we are concerned and we are following developments concerning the embargo on products from my country, which is having a direct and negative impact on our national economy at a difficult time like today. the effects and consequences of the russian blockade are chain-like. the surplus food will remain untapped, with the risk of crushing product prices. already humiliatingly low prices will be further reduced and the effects of speculation will be immediate and visible. it is unacceptable that we should target problem economies such as greece and pay the price of the russian-ukrainian crisis. the great political and business interests have started this crisis in our stead, always in the name of democracy and freedom, and we are now discussing solutions to repair the damage to cancer-stricken economies like my country's. third countries which are candidates for accession to the european union, and even nato members, which participate à la carte in european politics, benefit from the costs borne by the member states. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we need a new policy, a strategic approach between the european union and russia, we need to strengthen our partnership and trade relations. the events call for a review and a change in the policy being pursued. the continuation of the conflict with russia will bring more suffering and problems than benefits to the member states of the union.
we believe that sanctions were necessary to curb russian aggression, without forgetting the responsibilities of the european union and the united states of america in the maidan uprising. but wasn't russia's response expected? it was! the commission should have immediately had a plan in place to promote at least the vulnerable products in new markets, to promote them in the secondary sector or in schools, hospitals? where are the new upgraded instruments provided for in the just adopted common agricultural policy? why is the use of the crisis fund delayed, since the appropriations are there? the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's agricultural policy is not affected by the crisis. the economies of the south will not survive such a crisis without help. farmers are seeing their crops rot, and they will continue to see them rot because the crisis will last for over a year. the transfers, which no one has mentioned, are not only losing a percentage of their profits, but are in danger of being completely wiped out. finally, i would like to point out to the commissioner that if compensation is delayed by one year, there may be no farmers left to receive it and that time seems to pass differently for the commission and differently for farmers.
i would like to ask you, commissioner, whether the european union authorities, when they took these measures, were aware or not - had they foreseen or not - that there would be retaliation from russia? and how did they anticipate reacting to russia's countermeasures? secondly, did they or did they not know that these measures are mainly aimed at and affect the agricultural economy, that is to say the primary sector? did they or did they not know that the measures particularly affect greek producers who are fruit growers? fruit trees are not like broccoli and cabbage. it's not easy to destroy the tree and replace it with cabbage and broccoli. this is of enormous significance. i would also like to ask what funds are available and how much is being allocated to the crisis management fund. and most importantly, in this "coup de main" between russia and the european union, that is to say in the context of the atlantic alliance, who pays for the transport of nato troops to ukraine?

member of the commission. - mr president, honourable members of the european parliament, europe’s southern neighbourhood has gone through unprecedented change since 2011 and, in this context, trade and investment must play a role as a factor of stability: a factor of stability in your relations with the southern mediterranean region. we are currently focusing on three trade objectives vis-à-vis the southern mediterranean region. the first one is the deepening of eu trade relations with some of these partners on the basis of a differentiated approach. in december 2011 the council adopted negotiating directives for deep and comprehensive free trade areas (dcftas) with egypt, jordan, morocco and tunisia. the main objective of these agreements will be the progressive integration of the economies of these partners into the eu single market. these agreements will be comprehensive and will cover regulatory areas of mutual interest such as trade facilitation, sanitary and phytosanitary measures, public procurement and competition policy. they will also include investment protection and further liberalisation of goods, services and establishments, as appropriate. negotiations on a dcfta were launched with morocco in march 2013 and are progressing apace. the preparatory process for the negotiations with jordan and tunisia is very advanced, and negotiations could be launched as soon as the preparatory process is completed. the immediate priority for countries where a dcfta is not immediately possible – such as libya, lebanon or algeria – is their accession to the world trade organisation, and contacts continue in this respect. we also need to define the most appropriate trade framework for our relations with libya in due course. the second focus is support for south-south regional integration. the level of intra-regional trade in the southern mediterranean is still lower than anywhere else in the world. an integrated mediterranean market will be much more attractive to investors. we continue to support efforts to reinforce the agadir agreement between egypt, jordan, morocco and tunisia and to extend it to other regulatory areas: to trade and services, investments or public procurement. this also includes providing financial assistance and expertise. the ongoing revision of pan-euromed rules of origin should be accelerated. this is not an easy task, given the diverging views of the partners participating in this process. the third key element is the regional euro-mediterranean trade partnership, which will be revitalised at the upcoming 10th union for the mediterranean trade ministerial meeting on 30 september. this is an important meeting, since it will be the first in four years. the commission is doing its utmost to keep the trade relationship with the eu’s mediterranean neighbours up to date and to sustain long-term peace and stability in the region. we count on parliament’s continued dedication to this shared goal.

mr president, a large part of greece's public debt is held by the european central bank, which during the crisis bought greek government bonds on the secondary market at a discount of at least 40%. the ecb has thus paid eur 35 billion, but is still demanding the nominal value of these bonds, which is eur 53 billion. that's a profit of 22 billion euros. in this way mr draghi and his gang, by selling 'aircraft', are profiting unrestrainedly and uncompromisingly at the expense of the impoverished greek people. for this year alone, the greek people will pay a "hat" to the ecb of 4 billion euros and, for this reason, the memorandum government in athens has imposed with enfia unbearable taxes on the real estate of the greeks. the role of the ecb is not to speculate at the expense of the citizens of the european union. it is therefore both legal and ethical to 'purchase' these ecb bonds and to have greece pay only the principal paid by the ecb to buy these bonds, plus an interest rate of 0.15%.

answer is "blue card". mr president, the group's proposal has been analysed by mr lucke and others. the basic idea is that with this austerity policy currently being pursued by the european central bank and the european union, there can be no way out: we cannot have a europe that is full of unemployed people, we cannot have a europe in which banks are speculating, we cannot have a europe where liquidity is not going into the real economy, we cannot have a europe that is oppressing the people of the south, a europe that has now dismantled the whole economy in the south and is full of unemployed people. we need a different europe, a europe of nation states, which can really provide a way out of this situation, and not a europe of central governments, a europe which oppresses its own peoples.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen of the council and the commission, you have come empty-handed! mr president, ladies and gentlemen of the italian presidency, where is the turn to growth and change that mr renzi promised us here in june? mr katainen, where is the 300 billion euro programme of private and public investment that mr juncker told us about? you have not been able to tell us when the summit on growth will take place, and at the same time mrs merkel's economic orthodoxy is being questioned even by her own people. mr draghi is taking quantitative easing measures, timidly and belatedly, despite the obstacles put in his way by mr schäuble. even the oecd of mr guria, the neo-liberal, is telling you to stop this destructive policy, to take measures of quantitative easing and to turn to growth. france unilaterally announces that after two years it will comply with the requirements of the stability pact and you are telling us unbearably boring generalities, at a time when there is very high unemployment, 30% in my country, 60% unemployment among young people in greece, and at a time when the eurozone is facing a new recession and the risk of deflation. we expect answers from you, not generalities. the european parliament is not to be belittled or mocked.
mr president, i think that reforms are necessary because without reforms the european union will be economically defeated by the united states, china and other powerful competitors. i also believe that fiscal austerity is necessary, because otherwise we will fall into a black hole of debt, and we in greece are now paying, not so much for the difficult policy of the memorandum, as for the mistakes we made in the previous decade 2000-2009, when instead of preparing for additional competition within the framework of the emu - as, for example, our german friends had in the "agenda 2010" - we made provisions and we missed the budgetary management. but it takes an extra effort, a creative thought, to fill in the body of the policy that is basically right. we must first of all be clear about what we want. how strong do we want the euro to be? do we want inflation close to 0% or inflation close to 2%? do we want liquidity, do we want quantitative easing or do we want monetary orthodoxy? i think we can do it, but we have to try, there's no automation that's going to solve the problem. finally, i would like to protest against certain eurosceptics, especially the germans, who are criticizing greece and calling for the south to be abandoned. do they not understand that if we fall into a black hole of debt we will drag in the german taxpayers, whom they are supposed to represent?

mr president, the deadly ebola virus has been ravaging africa for many years. and yet, as long as the virus was confined to africa, the civilized west remained defiantly indifferent. the european union and its member states have been indifferent when it came to investing resources in the ebola response. now the epidemic is on the doorstep of the european union, the united states and elsewhere. now the risks of possible mutation of the virus are increased. now the ebola victims are increasing at a geometrical rate. the european union has finally woken up. so urgent measures are needed. the 500 million euros that the european union has promised to release are not enough for the african countries that have been impoverished by exploitation. the voluntary organizations that are on the front lines of the battle in africa, where virtually all health structures have been destroyed, must be strengthened. the research needs to be intensified to find the vaccine for ebola. in the member states of the european union there must be decisive and urgent preparation at all levels, with emphasis on increasing hospital beds for medical care, and all this at a time when the memoranda have imposed drastic cuts in health expenditure in the states of the european south. finally, border controls must be increased and thermal cameras installed at all airports and ports to protect local communities from the deadly haemorrhagic fever.

madam president, 'in the same play spectators'. we hear the same thing over and over again. we see at the last summit in milan that the european union continues to base its competitiveness on the 'chineseisation' of wages, i.e. on the reduction of wages, a wrong policy, while it must instead invest in new technologies, education and training. but how can it do that when by violent budgetary adjustment it cuts public spending on education itself? it is therefore still relying on austerity, whereas it must boost demand. he continues to support the bank bailout: 1.6 trillion euros were given to bail out the banks, that is, to socialize the losses of the banks. only 6 billion euros for the youth guarantee. another trillion euros from mr draghi, again for the banks. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
in writing. i welcome the conclusions of the employment summit in europe, held in milan on the initiative of the italian presidency. the unemployment in the eu, especially among young people, women and the disabled, is scandalous. the eu has taken initiatives to boost employment from the structural funds, mainly through the european social fund, but also through the youth initiative, which gives new opportunities to apprenticeships and skills development. but unfortunately, these actions are not enough. the european union is a major player in the world economy, particularly in the european south, which is suffering from the economic crisis and the unprecedented unemployment rates. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament on the subject of the future of the european union. we need new, targeted lines of financing which will help to revive the european economy and preserve social cohesion. it is the responsibility of all of us to build a stable framework that fosters public and private investment, creativity, start-ups, lifelong learning and mobility. the eu is one of the largest economies in the world, and our young people and human resources, taken together, are the best it has ever had. we must strengthen the competitiveness of the european market and keep our young people within our borders.
in writing. the milan employment summit has shown that the eu is not only unwilling but also unable to tackle the problem of unemployment, which is worsening and afflicting millions of families. the european union policy is spreading unemployment to more people, making flexible working more widespread, especially among young people, giving employers free labour, subsidising businesses and providing false training, slashing wages and fighting for rights that young people are seeking to never know. the aim of the eu and the parties that support it is to reduce the demands of workers, especially young people. the eu's 'fundamental principles' of free movement of capital and labour mobility ensure cheap labour for large groups, while at the same time throwing thousands of workers out of work. despite the competition, the eu's common objective is to make this situation permanent, to ensure that the eu's citizens are able to enjoy the benefits of the single market. this system cannot guarantee the right to work. it needs the armies of unemployed to ensure cheap labour, a basic requirement for profitability, competitiveness. securing the right to full, permanent work requires organized struggle, overthrowing the system that produces unemployment, poverty and exploitation.
the european semester is a tool which seeks to achieve coordination in the implementation of a failed policy, which is measured by certain figures. investment in the euro area is 15% lower than in 2009. the youth unemployment rate is 25%, while in my country, greece, it has reached 60%. the eurozone is stagnating and threatened with recession, so your theory, mr katainen, that 'hard unilateral austerity will bring investment' has failed. and mr della vedova, the italian presidency is coming to an end, it is the end of october, and the very nice words that mr renzi spoke to us here on 1 july about major changes are still awaited. what did you achieve at the unemployment summit in milan? a whole lot of nothing. mr katainen, you said that you could not tell us anything about the famous 300 billion euros promised to us by mr juncker on 1 july in this house because you were new. you were too old as a representative of a harsh neoliberal austerity. your policy has failed.
the report on the european semester, mr president, supports the anti-people measures to reduce the income of the people, to abolish the rights of workers and of the social security system. it calls for the unwavering application of the memoranda of understanding, the financing of banks and monopoly groups. the people are paying a heavy price for the capitalist crisis and the anaemic growth, however it comes, will be marked by a continuation of the anti-people offensive. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european union's financial instruments are used to support the development of the internal market, and i would like to thank the commission for its efforts in this area. the parties which present the fiscal relaxation as a popular recipe are mocking the workers, they are the very carriers of this anti-people attack. it is imperative that the people's struggle to recover their losses, to overturn the anti-people policy, to abolish exploitation, should now be developed.
mr president, the european semester for the coordination of economic policies is an undemocratic instrument for controlling the budgets of the member states in the context of german europe. in fact, greece will remain under continuous enhanced surveillance until it has repaid 75% of its debt to the european union, that is, at current rates, until 2050. greece can only exit enhanced supervision if it claims the repayment of germany's debts to greece, i.e. eur 108 billion without interest for german reparations and eur 54 billion without interest for the occupation loan, i.e. eur 162 billion without interest. we therefore call once again on the greek government to enter in the state budget the total amount of germany's debts to greece, which are both due and receivable and interest, amounting to eur 162 billion. because greece is the creditor and germany is the debtor. the european parliament has already expressed its views on the situation in the former yugoslavia, and i would like to thank the commission for its support for the efforts of the greek government to bring about a peaceful solution to the conflict.
mr president, i think the picture is not as bleak as some colleagues have suggested. in recent years there has been serious progress on the basis of joint effort, a characteristic success being the withdrawal of ireland from the memorandum and i would even say 'excellent'. our portuguese friends were able to get out of the memorandum, our cypriot friends are doing better than planning and i am optimistic that my country, greece, will finally succeed, provided that the government becomes more bold in reforms, is able to reduce taxes by financing this reduction with a corresponding reduction in public spending, and i would also tell my friends in the greek opposition to become more responsible, more creative. i think we know what the general issues are, we need budgetary discipline - there is no other way - we need structural changes, we have understood this, and we also need a boost to investment activity, whether that boost comes through public spending from countries - of course - that have the means, or from countries that can reduce current spending and go ahead with public investment, and of course we also need the juncker plan or similar initiatives. so i am optimistic, as long as we can get along and move forward.

in writing. the globalisation fund is unwilling and unable to tackle the huge problem of unemployment. it is being used to cover up anti-people politics, the exploitative nature of capitalist enterprises and the eu itself, by funding programmes to recycle unemployment and pseudo-training mechanisms that squander funds. the peugeot and ford cases are typical. monopoly groups exploit the capitalist crisis to reduce wages and throw thousands of workers out of work, moving the factories that are closing to countries with cheaper labor. the increase in the exploitation of workers is a necessary condition for the monopolies to maintain their profitability, even in the midst of the crisis, and to strengthen their position in the world monopoly competition that is raging. peugeot workers, ford workers, the working class as a whole must not limit their demands to the extreme poverty management trifles thrown at them by the eu and bourgeois governments. it needs to organise its struggle to regroup and counterattack the labour movement, targeting the very exploitative system that generates unemployment and poverty, to claim ownership of the wealth it produces, taking into its hands the keys to the economy and power.

mr president, i think that the management of european union money is very good quality and efficient, and i disagree with the british eurosceptics and british anti-europeans who specialize in criticising alleged waste in the european union. the management criteria in the european union are much stricter, because we have a budget deficit limit, in emu, of 3% of gnp, whereas in the united kingdom, for example, the corresponding deficit is in the order of 6% of gnp. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i think we are a bit lazy in the budgetary approach. we must support the positions of the european parliament because we are facing new challenges. if, for example, ukraine collapses in winter because we have been indifferent, will we have saved money or will we have opened the way to new spending? and if, for example, ebola escapes from west africa because we've also been neglectful, and it comes to the european union, what are we going to gain financially, lose financially, do we meet to decide that, or do we talk through teleconferencing? in any case, i think that the italian presidency has responsibilities, mr renzi came here with good ideas, at least he should help us to uphold the prestige of the european parliament and the european union.
mr president, in a few moments we shall be called upon to ratify the election of the commissioners of the european union. we all know that the commissioner's role is very important, because he is called upon to implement the european union's programme and policy in the field he chairs and coordinates. the way in which the commissioner is chosen and elected is not flattering to the member. what 's the deal ? the commissioner has been selected by the country in cooperation with the president of the european commission and we are then simply asked to vote for or against him. we are therefore taking part in a formal procedure which we cannot influence and in which we cannot intervene. this would be done if there were at least two candidates for each position. that is what we are suggesting, and until that happens, we will simply have the illusion that we are participating in a process over which we have no influence.
the new commission, mr president, will continue, as did the previous commission, to attack the peoples with unabated intensity. the european union is an association of capital, so its institutions, their mission and their functioning, regardless of who they are, are marked by the rights of workers and serve the business groups and their ever-increasing profitability. the appointment of the new commission was made through bargaining and competition which have nothing to do with the interests of the working people. the programme statements, like the commissioners' hearings, are a means of promoting the european union's long-standing anti-union policy. the communist party of greece votes against the new european commission and calls on the people, without any waiting, without any delay, to organize the militant response for immediate recovery of losses, to chart a path of development that serves its interests in conflict with the european union and the monopolies.
mr president, mr juncker, both in your speech of 15 july and today you have shown that you have great ambitions. i agree and i sign it. we shall support you personally and the commission with all our strength in this endeavour. we want more and better europe. we want to broaden and deepen our democratic institutions. the european union is an incomplete institution, both economically and politically. if the united states has made it through the global crisis at the lowest possible cost, it is because it has a federal budget six times larger than that of the european union. they may print even a little inflationary money during the crisis. they have the federal bonds, we have nothing. we don't even have a policy to print one and two euro notes. but also on a political level, what is our effectiveness on the issue of ukraine? what is the effectiveness for kobani in the crisis of syria and iraq? what is our effectiveness in terms of aggression, of the violation of the exclusive economic zone of cyprus by turkey? is the exclusive economic zone of the member states an exclusive economic zone of the european union or not? for all these challenges, for all these difficulties we stand by your side and we will help you with all our strength.
i am not going to vote for mr juncker and his committee. the commission which you have just presented to us is a commission in which the old political world of europe is represented. four former prime ministers, several former ministers, all those who are politically responsible for the impoverishment of europe, for the socialization of the losses of the banks, for the creation of a huge army of unemployed, for the chineseisation of wages, for the over-indebtedness of greece with a huge and humiliating debt, so those who failed now promise to save europe, after having literally dismantled it. we are totally opposed to the commission's programme, because it condemns the european south to second gear, it strengthens german europe, it undermines the europe of the creditors who support the troika and the memoranda in greece, and it does not oppose the merkel policy.
