in writing. the eu-moldova association agreement and the accompanying free trade agreement serve the ambitions of eu monopolies and sections of the country's domestic capital that see their interests better served by relations with the eu. these agreements accelerate the liberalisation of the moldovan market through a series of capitalist restructuring measures that facilitate the penetration of monopoly groups, such as large-scale privatisations, the award of public contracts to foreign companies, and the functioning of the public administration to serve 'investors'. the conclusion of the agreements with moldova is part of the imperialist plans of the so-called 'eastern partnership', the eu's strategy for its penetration into countries which were former soviet republics and which today are the scene of fierce confrontation between the eu and capitalist russia, with the exploitation of their rich resource-producing, especially energy, resources as the focus. these agreements and the monopoly competition between capitalist associations are intensifying the exploitation of the people of moldova and the other peoples of the region, and are creating the risk of armed conflict and bloodshed, as the experience of ukraine shows. the peoples' interest lies not in choosing the imperialist camp but in clashing with the monopolies and their power to take their fate into their own hands.

mr president, the draft resolution we are discussing sets out objectives for global development after 2015, such as eradicating poverty, combating inequalities and respecting human rights, on which there should be broad agreement. i must point out, however, that the attitude of the main political forces in this respect, both in the european parliament and in the governments of the member states, is very hypocritical, since opposite policies are being pursued within the union. in the member states with excessive debt in particular, the notorious structural reforms are being translated into the violent impoverishment of the majority of society, to the benefit of domestic and especially international capital, and into a systematic violation of social rights. i therefore believe that instead of making grandiose declarations about the future of other continents, the european union should look at itself in the mirror and change course before it is too late.
mr president, in a period of enormous economic and technological development of the world society, there are 1.2 billion of our fellow men living in conditions of extreme poverty. at the same time, 250,000 people across the planet have a net worth of $30 trillion, or over one-third of global gdp. according to a recent ubs report, the trend of the rich getting richer and the poor getting poorer continued in 2014. the total net wealth of the rich americans increased by 6%, the rich europeans by 8.9% and the rich greeks, in troika and memorandum greece, by 16.7%, from $60 billion to $70 billion. at the same time, 3.9 million people in greece live below the poverty line, i.e. 35.7% compared to 27.6% in 2009. so poverty is the result of specific policies - the policies of the memoranda and austerity.
mr president, the controls on exports of dual-use goods are an important action which the european union must encourage. this can also be done at international level through mechanisms for the adoption of common practices, also known as export control regimes. the issue of new members joining export control regimes must be considered on a technical basis and not as a field for the exercise of exclusionary policies. in this context, it should be noted that turkey is blocking the candidacy of cyprus to the mtcr missile technology control regime, the wassenaar arrangement and the zangger commission. the european union should therefore support the cypriot candidacy for these export control regimes by putting pressure on turkey.
on behalf of the ecr group. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment is a very important one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done. this is a very important political issue concerning the european union's own competences, the protection of the principle of delegation and the protection of the presumption of competence in favour of the member states. this is the first element that the european commission should consider. the second is respect for fundamental rights. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the third point concerns the impact on small and mediumsized enterprises, which really must be taken very seriously. i will tell you, for example, what is happening in greece, where the memorandum is being implemented and where no controls have been carried out and no economic and social impact assessment has been carried out, where we have thousands of unemployed people, where we have thousands of impoverished greeks. i would like to tell you, commissioner, what has happened to small and mediumsized enterprises, because the commission is a member of the troika. two hundred and thirty thousand businesses have closed. there was a 75% drop in turnover. the tax liabilities cannot be met. 46% of smes are late in making payments. if you had done a social and economic impact assessment, we might have had different results. we must also have an impact assessment on technological development because without technology we cannot move to the new production model. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
madam president, sixty-nine years have passed since the end of the second world war, and yet germany refuses to pay greece the war reparations awarded to it by the international conference in paris in 1946, amounting to 108 billion euros, excluding interest. it also refuses to sign a peace treaty with greece, even though both countries are members of the european union. at the same time, germany refuses to return to my country the forced occupation loan it imposed in 1942 on occupied greece, which today amounts to 54 billion euros, without interest. this loan destroyed the greek economy and over 600,000 greeks died of starvation. the european parliament has also expressed its concern at the situation in the former yugoslavia, which is a source of concern for the european union. all this is contained in the black book published by my colleague, mr manolis glezos. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european parliament is able to exercise its right to vote.
madam president, democracy in greece is being systematically violated when its citizens living abroad cannot vote in national elections, and especially when, because of the crisis, there is a wave of immigration, mainly of young people. the greek state is preventing all of them from exercising their democratic rights, as the cost of travel is prohibitive. so only the rich will vote? why is the greek government deaf to the calls of the greek communities and not allowing expatriate greeks to vote from their countries of residence, when they are voting in the european elections? why does the greek government not respect the relevant resolutions of the european parliament and not apply an earlier relevant law? why is the european union not putting pressure on the greek government when it is instead doing its bit to save the banks? who's afraid of who in this crisis?
madam president, in recent years in europe, a large proportion of our fellow citizens have been living below the poverty line because of the austerity policies and fiscal adjustment measures implemented in many member states of the european union to deal with the economic crisis. according to eurostat data for 2013, 120 million europeans live under this regime. my country, greece, ranks third in europe for poverty rates, and unemployment is over 27%. this fact reveals the depth of the crisis. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full, and that the community's financial resources are used to the full. europe must now tackle the spread of poverty effectively and must pursue a long-term strategy with the aim of gradually restoring the living standards of europeans, especially in the countries where the phenomenon is particularly acute.
madam president, the resolution and the 2020 strategy of the european union and the urban governments for cheap labor and capital gains mandate subsidies and the transfer of taxes and contributions from employers to the people who pay with savage taxes and with reductions in wages, pensions and social benefits. the generalization of flexible low-paid work spreads unemployment to more people. there is: subordination of training to business planning; unpaid work with training and apprenticeship programmes; generalised poverty line with minimum guaranteed income; the commission has also proposed that the council of ministers should adopt a decision on the establishment of a european company law body. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a common position on the proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens at work. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect, and i am sure that the commission will be able to support them. this policy cannot have social aspects; the european union is a cause of war against capital, against the european union and against the parties which support and embellish it. the general strike on november 27th in greece will give an answer.

in writing. - i vote against the recommendation on the draft council decision approving, on behalf of the european union, the hague convention of 30 june 2005 on agreements on the extension of jurisdiction, because a company such as carrefour can impose in advance on a small farmer from greece that the exclusive place of settlement of their disputes may be a country other than greece and germany. in the past, a case could be tried in the countries involved, and the advantage was to be the first to act legally. the agreement allows the large company to require the other party to specify the place of jurisdiction during the conclusion of the agreement. the seat may be set in mexico or in an eu country to which the transition would be unprofitable for the small party or which tends, by its laws or system, to defend the big interests. with this agreement, a greek producer may not be able to bring a case in his own country.

madam president, i would be permitted to sit in my seat and not stand because of my condition. the evidence for physical and psychological violence is overwhelming: some 13 million women report being victims of physical violence, or 7% of european women aged 18 to 74. violence against women has many faces, it knows no borders or classes, it is inflicted in cruel and ruthless ways, it is experienced within the family, in society and in recent years very often in the workplace. i personally experienced brutal violence to stop the demands of a class of people and to make their voices unheard. but now, a new wave of economic violence is reigniting in greece. an employer asked a pregnant employee to terminate her pregnancy if she wanted to have a job. the commission has already taken the necessary steps to ensure that the community's budgetary resources are used to the full, and that the budgetary resources available are used to the full. we must respond to the violence that is so unprovoked even against motherhood with legislation in conjunction with the ratification of the council of europe convention, the well-known istanbul convention. we must create an effective legal protection framework.
in writing. it's sad that it's 2014 and we're still talking about violence against women. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the rights of women are protected, and that the rights of women are protected in the european union. the 25th of november should not be a day to see the problem grow but to deal with it and reduce it. the eu must focus on the first of the two approaches, prevention and repression. it is absolutely essential that the istanbul convention be ratified by all member states and that new legislation be promoted to further protect the rights of battered women. but repressive policies follow crime when it has already been committed. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives to promote the development of the european audiovisual industry. fortunately, the phenomenon of domestic violence is the exception to the rule. we must therefore systematically ensure that there is specialised training at all stages of education, which analyses the causes of violence, the profile of perpetrators/victims and the circumstances in which violence is formed. this will create a culture against violence and a radical change in mindset.

mr president, commissioner, the nutritional status of children under five is one of the indicators of family well-being and one of the determinants of child survival. child malnutrition is one of the most important causes of infant and child mortality. it can adversely affect the child's intellectual development and, consequently, his health and productivity in later life. it is also one of the criteria recommended by the world health organization for assessing health equality. unfortunately for europe, it will also have to include new areas of malnutrition that are suffering from the effects of austerity policies to deal with the economic crisis. as we speak, in greece, children are fainting in school during class time because of malnutrition. thousands of greek families, with zero income due to unemployment, cannot provide for their children. children go to school fasting. teachers and professors, on their meager salaries due to commemorative cuts, give money to the cafeterias to offer discreet snacks to children who they know cannot afford to buy. but these, out of pride or even shame, refuse to accept it. instead of giving our children the quality of life they deserve, we are bringing european countries to the living conditions of sub-saharan africa through the wrong policies and the wrong economic measures.
mr president, the title of this report, child malnutrition, is a revealing confession. we all agree that there are children who are malnourished. everything that's been heard, all the findings are correct. but it's not enough to just find out. we need to get to the cause. why is malnutrition occurring, especially among children? the reason is the system that has its god in money. let's not be too blind. the system is the capitalist regime, it is the global authoritarian regime, it is the g8-20, it is the regime of wars against peoples, nations, citizens, it is the regime of arms manufacture and arms trade. i was surprised the pope didn't launch a campaign against gun control yesterday. the working population, say the scientists, if there were no such system, would only need to work one minute a day to meet all its needs and put an end to this situation.

a proposal for a council decision to accede to the convention on international trade in endangered species of wild fauna and flora will be adopted in the coming days. the need to vote in favour of the proposal becomes apparent when one realizes that since the early 1970s, people, for commercial gain, have been caught up in an inexplicable mania for destroying many species of plants and animals that are in danger of disappearing from the planet. it is estimated that by 2020 about 10,000,000 of these plants and animals will have disappeared. if we add to this the loss of animals, mainly due to man's destruction of forests, pastures, coral reefs and pollution of the seas, which is also man-made, then this number of animals is near the tragic limit. the european union is therefore faced with a very important challenge, which it must successfully meet. it starts with the best intentions. but he asks: will it succeed in curbing the hitherto uncontrolled activity of legal and illegal traders of these species? will he be able to control the trade of these items via the internet? the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this field, and i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is prepared to take the necessary steps to ensure that the commission's proposals are implemented in the medium term. i hope we succeed in this endeavour.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european union's fisheries policy is not disrupted by the introduction of new species of fish. there are thousands of species protected by cites and through this convention we can hit the smugglers and the various circles that profit from the trade in endangered animals. the role of the convention is therefore positive and i believe that the european union should accede to it. the way in which the european union's accession will be carried out must, however, fully respect the responsibilities of the member states within the scope of the treaty. at the same time, this should also apply in the future, and the commission should not try to indirectly extend the powers of the european union to the detriment of the powers of the member states, as it usually does. the commission must therefore be under constant control and accountable to the european parliament. mr president, i think there is a danger of confusing the mechanisms with the substance, that is to say, of criticizing the 'two-pack-six-pack', of wanting to break the thermometer in order not to see the fever that warns us of a possible economic or budgetary disease. i think we're doing well overall. there are certain successes: ireland has left the memorandum, portugal has left the memorandum, latvia and lithuania have done a good job and got their ticket to the euro, cyprus is doing well overall, from what i have been told; i am sorry to say that my country, greece, is still trying but has not succeeded. but i will tell you that the problems of greece, because i was a journalist and publisher for 35 years, i followed them before the euro and before the two-pack-six-pack and before the stabilization mechanisms, and i will tell you that they were basically the same. i mean, we were giving out big pensions, we had an overly rich welfare state that couldn't be financed by the private sector of the economy, and i'll tell our british anti-european and eurosceptic friends, who say that in greece we have huge problems because of the euro, that we used to give out pensions in greece that were 100 or even 120 percent of the last salary, whereas in the united kingdom pensions - because i read about it - were in the range of 35 to 40 percent of the last salary. the problems were therefore already there. we cannot attribute them to the euro. on the contrary, the euro makes it easier for us to restructure our debt in the future, it makes it easier, as in the case of ireland, to reduce the cost of borrowing in the future, while, with the measures taken by mr draghi, the euro is becoming less strong and it also makes it easier for countries like greece to export. madam president, an earthquake in my country, greece, which caused damage of some 147 million euros, with more than two and a half thousand houses unable to house their families, led the european solidarity union to provide some 3.5 million euros in aid. this action shows the true face of the european union. a union which creates rules and laws, to apply them when and where it suits. a union that thinks of the economic interests of the powerful states, while leaving the other countries with crumbs. a union which cannot and does not want to think that a country suffering from a huge economic crisis, a country where unemployment has reached an inhumane level and almost half of its population lives below the poverty line, cannot assess and follow the same rules as countries where similar conditions do not exist. the obvious economic and political racism of the budget and the social deficit of the dominant powers are deeply and perpendicularly at odds with the founding declaration of the union. let us all remember the principles on which the european union was built: equality, mutual cooperation and respect.

the cia torture is not surprising. the united states and its intelligence agencies to date have a rich history of imperialist interventions, junta, coups, savage torture, cold-blooded murder of african americans. the european union has long been seen as the champion of human rights. the common foreign and security policy, europol, eurojustice, all the european union and nato repression mechanisms are partners and allies of the cia. the member states of the european union are cooperating with the united states in many areas of repression of the popular labour movement. they also cooperate in state and industrial espionage, telephone and internet surveillance, and cover up inhumane torture. all of this is not a contradiction of the capitalist system but its true face. their goal is to defend capitalist exploitation against the enemy people. this is the dictatorship of the monopolies and the people can make their own decisions to overthrow it with their own power. they have the power.
the inhumane practices used by the cia under president bush to interrogate detainees are evidence of the real lack of respect for human rights that characterizes the dominant political forces in the united states. on the one hand, they are acting as vigilantes, issuing annual reports on the situation of fundamental rights in third countries, while on the other hand they are themselves in flagrant violation of the convention against torture which they signed in 1994. the union's attitude is not much different. while the european union claims to protect fundamental rights, in practice, in recent years, it has systematically trampled on them, especially social rights, through the imposition of devastating austerity policies. long-term unemployment, lack of medical care, inability to heat homes in winter, all amount to economic torture. the european governments promised the peoples of europe a paradise and are serving up hell. change course before you destroy europe!
in writing. the modern international and european legal arsenal is undoubtedly based on an unshakable central pillar: the absolute protection of human rights, which includes the explicit prohibition of the use of torture for any reason. the debate on this subject is unfortunately opened up each time by some unpleasant revelation, such as the fact that european soil has recently been used by the cia to torture terrorism suspects into confessing. although the european union, through its institutional and legal instruments, has repeatedly condemned such behaviour, one can reasonably wonder what is happening behind closed doors, especially in view of the intensified fight against terrorism and the attempt to prevent a terrorist attack in europe. the european parliament has been a key player in the development of the european union's policy on torture, and it has been a key player in the development of the european union's policy on torture. it seems that despite our strict legal tradition, which is totally opposed to such practices, we must also be vigilant in the eu about the self-evident right to protection against any form of state or police violence, which may ultimately be exercised with our tolerance or ignorance, but which makes us complicit. in the europe of justice, torture is neither acceptable nor should it be allowed or even condoned.

recently erdogan said that he does not accept lessons in democracy from europe. once upon a time, a farmer, in winter, found a frozen snake, took pity on it, took it and put it in his hive. the snake at some point, after warming up and regaining consciousness, bit its benefactor. when he breathed his last, he said, "it is good for me to be in this condition, since i have shown mercy to a criminal". the above-mentioned tale is from the myths of aesop. but it is fully in line with today's reality. i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter, and whether it is not in a position to do so. the european union is a country of extreme authoritarianism, which does not respect the rights of individuals and states and ignores the decisions of international organizations! so don't be surprised by the lack of freedom of expression in turkey and the persecution of media and journalists. the blood of the anti-government protesters in taksim square in istanbul has not yet dried. ask, read, research and realize: illegal invasion and occupation of part of cyprus, a member state of the european union, and aggression in its eez; genocide of the ponts and the armenians; the european union is committed to the peace process and to the peace process in the region. the european union is also calling for the immediate release of the hostages and for the immediate release of the hostages of the kurdish people. the european union has a duty to ensure that the turkish authorities are not forced to accept the turkish people's demands. mr president, 200,000 people, most of them young people, have emigrated in recent years because they were persecuted by the regime in their country. economic migrants on the verge of poverty, unemployed, without decent medical care, leaving behind homes at risk of bank foreclosure. this country is not sudan, nor is it part of sub-saharan africa. it is greece, a member state of the european union. but unfortunately it has a lot in common with the country you're thinking of helping today. these 200,000 greeks, who have emigrated because of the crisis, are young scientists up to 40 years old. bright minds with a hunger for work but no future in my home country of greece. the european union has a duty to help the countries of the region to develop their economies and to ensure that they are able to cope with the challenges of the future. we know better, because we are dealing with it ourselves, faced with the european union, which is forcing us to accept illegal immigrants in our country, as the dublin ii treaty stipulates. but it is foolish for the european union to fund programmes to restrict immigration from africa when it is not interested in reducing internal migration caused by its bad policies. the internal migration flow proves that the countries of the vigorous north, condemning the countries of the south to economic withering and impoverishment, attract the most valuable human resources.
the commissioner, in order to limit the flow of migrants from the countries of the horn of africa, must eliminate the root causes of this problem, which force people to leave these areas. and of course, the first and most important thing is to overthrow the dictatorial regimes that exist in the countries of the horn of africa, to restore and support democracy there, to stop the persecution, to protect human rights. secondly, there must be peace in the region, because as long as we have a state of war in various places, problems are created and, obviously, people have to leave, they have to save their lives. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, including the establishment of a european centre for the development of vocational training (ecvet) and the establishment of a european centre for vocational training (ecvet). the commission has already taken the necessary steps to ensure that the european union's financial interests are protected. the european union must also make a contribution to the development of the european union, and in particular to the development of the european union's relations with the countries of the south. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them.
the commission is therefore proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: mr president, the bloody conflicts in venezuela this year began with the attempt by the far right to overthrow the democratically elected government of maduro by force. instead of the political forces in power in the european parliament dismissing the venezuelan government, and even selectively using elements from the report of the united nations high commissioner for human rights, al-hussein, they would do better to deal with the corresponding report of the united nations high commissioner for human rights, sefas lumina, which finds systematic violations of fundamental rights in greece, the memorandum, because of the policies of austerity imposed by the troika and ecofin. and instead of lessons in democracy in venezuela, they had better show more respect for democratic procedures in the member states of the union and stop trying to blackmail the consciences of greek parliamentarians and voters with the threats of bankruptcy, exit from the eurozone and other similar ones that we have heard in recent days. i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply. (the president accepted the question with a blue card) (rule 162 (8) of the rules of procedure)
answer to a question on the 'blue card' procedure. - mr buchner, i have to go. terror and torture are wrong no matter where they come from. but we have legal proceedings here against people who have committed crimes in venezuela.
mr president, it may be hard to believe, but in the 21st century, more than 20% of the population of mauritania are slaves. the government of the country, instead of trying to deal with this unacceptable situation, did something simpler: he arrested mr. abed, the president of an anti-slavery activist organization, on baseless charges as well as some of the members of his organization. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. but when the same attitude is adopted by a government of a democratic country, such as the government of greece, which, following the brutal intervention of the prime minister in the judiciary, has illegally kept in prison for 14 months the leader, members of parliament and officials of greece's third largest political party, golden dawn, why do we not show the same sensitivity? the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. but i mention it to show how much hypocrisy there is in this room. if you think i'm wrong and i'm wronging you, please by your behavior refute me.
the resolution is a resolution on human rights, without political overtones, as was the previous resolution on venezuela. slavery is widespread in mauritania and the persecution of human rights activists and biram dah abeid must stop. i therefore condemn the slavery that exists in mauritania. i support the resolution, and the european parliament is rightly concerned with the protection of human rights. but it must do the same for the violation of human rights in the european union itself, in the countries which are under a memorandum; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council and parliament. we have reached the point where the commission has said, in answer to a question, that it cannot respect human rights in the case of greece because the memorandum is being applied there. it is unacceptable and we condemn it. i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply.
in writing. i voted against the motion to reject the request of the philippines, which is engaged in illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing. in addition, the philippines illegally uses mainly women and children for forced labour in fishing.
in writing. the commission has been given a yellow card and has opened proceedings against the philippines for illegal, unreported and unregulated fishing. the un also notes that the trafficking of people, especially women and children, for the purpose of forced labour is widespread in various sectors, such as agriculture, construction, fisheries, processing and services, in the philippines.
in writing. i voted in favour of the motion for a resolution on the commission delegated regulation of 19 august amending annex iii to regulation (eu) no 978/2012 of the european parliament and of the council applying a scheme of generalised tariff preferences, as it deals adequately and appropriately with the subject matter it regulates.
in writing. i voted against the proposal, as i am opposed to the existence of the single resolution fund and to advance contributions to financial resolution arrangements.
in writing. we generally disagree with the existence of the single resolution fund and advance contributions to financial resolution arrangements.
in writing. i voted against the recommendation for a decision not to raise objections to the commission delegated regulation of 21 october 2014 supplementing directive 2014/59/eu of the european parliament and of the council of 15 may 2014 on ex ante contributions to financial restructuring arrangements, as i do not agree with the delegation of power to the commission in this matter. moreover, that recommendation does not adequately address the issues it purports to address.
in writing. we generally disagree with the existence of the single resolution fund and advance contributions to financial resolution arrangements.
in writing. i voted against the report because enlargement of the eu by admitting the economically weakest serves only the interests of the economically strongest states, without solving the crisis affecting the countries of the south, and in the long run will have economic and social consequences for the acceding states similar to those experienced by our country.
in writing. the report should be voted on, as enlargement of the eu to include the economically weakest states serves only the interests of the economically strong states, without resolving the crisis affecting the countries of the south, and in the long term will have economic and social consequences for the acceding states similar to those experienced by our country.
in writing. i abstained from the vote on the mamikins report on the draft council decision on the signing, on behalf of the european union, of the association agreement between the european union and the european atomic energy community and their member states, of the one part, and georgia, of the other part, because i do not consider that the political, geostrategic and economic conditions for the conclusion and subsequent application of the agreement have matured.
in writing. at the present time, when the economic crisis is still ongoing, economic agreements with third countries on terms favourable to them do not contribute to solving the economic problems of the member states; it's actually making them worse.
in writing. i voted against the recommendation, as the enlargement of trade partners (with third countries looking to join the eu) serves only the interests of economically powerful states, without solving the crisis affecting the countries of the south, and in the long run will bring about economic consequences for the contracting third states equivalent to those experienced by our country.
in writing. it is proposed that the recommendation be voted on, as the enlargement of trade partners (with third countries seeking eu membership) serves only the interests of economically powerful states, without resolving the crisis affecting the countries of the south, and in the long term will have economic consequences for the contracting third states corresponding to those experienced by our country.
in writing. i abstained from the vote on the mamikins report on the draft council decision on the signing, on behalf of the european union, of the association agreement between the european union and the european atomic energy community and their member states, of the one part, and georgia, of the other part, because i do not consider that the political, geostrategic and economic conditions for the conclusion and subsequent application of the agreement have matured.
in writing. at the present time, when the economic crisis is still ongoing, economic agreements with third countries on terms favourable to them do not contribute to solving the economic problems of the member states; it's actually making them worse.
